  • se una persona «limitata» modifica una pagina che contiene codice scritto da una persona «fidata», quel codice resta intatto e bloccato: non lo può cambiare né duplicare;
  • al contrario, un contenuto scritto da una persona limitata resta limitato anche quando un amministratore salva, pubblica o ripristina la pagina che lo contiene. Il codice non diventa mai «fidato» solo perché passa dalle mani giuste.

Le regole valgono su ogni strada: il builder, l'editor a blocchi, le importazioni di release e di siti Etch, gli agenti IA.

Nel builder non si scrive PHP, per scelta: sarebbe codice eseguito sul server a portata di chiunque modifichi i contenuti. Il PHP del sito, nei file del tema o di un plugin, si usa nel builder come dati e blocchi dinamici.

Cosa fa Histeon per la sicurezza #

Ecco le protezioni principali, in parole semplici.

Il builder è protetto.

  • La pagina del builder ha una politica di sicurezza dei contenuti (CSP) rigida: gira solo il codice di Histeon, e la pagina non si può incorporare in altri siti.
  • L'anteprima nella tela è isolata: gli script della pagina non girano, i link non navigano e i form non si inviano.
  • La tela carica immagini, video, font e fogli di stile solo dal sito (anche dalla sua CDN dei media) e dalle origini scritte in Histeon → Impostazioni → Origini esterne ammesse nella tela: così l'indirizzo di chi lavora non arriva a siti terzi. Un'immagine da un'altra origine si vede come un rettangolo grigio con l'origine nel suggerimento; nella pagina pubblicata resta com'è. Anche i font di un foglio esterno (per esempio Google Fonts) non arrivano nella tela: meglio scaricarli sul sito (Design system → Font del sito).
  • Le richieste del builder al sito sono controllate con i permessi dell'utente e con i codici di sicurezza di WordPress.

Niente va online per sbaglio.

  • Tutto quello che salvi va in sviluppo. Il sito cambia solo con una release, e ogni release si può ripristinare.
  • Ogni versione salvata ha un'impronta (SHA-256) che viene ricontrollata quando si legge. Una release alterata non viene ripristinata.
  • Histeon tiene un registro delle operazioni importanti: pubblicazioni e ripristini, importazioni, esportazioni, configurazioni, modifiche fatte dagli agenti IA. Gli amministratori lo leggono in Histeon → Registro, con i filtri per operazione, persona e giorni, e lo esportano in CSV.

Anteprime e sito statico non lasciano scappare nulla.

  • I link di anteprima sono firmati, scadono, valgono solo per quella pagina e quell'utente, e non vengono indicizzati. Si possono revocare tutti insieme.
  • Prima di pubblicare la copia statica su Cloudflare o di scaricarla come zip, Histeon la controlla: se trova dati privati o di sessione (percorsi del server, codici di sicurezza, token di anteprima), si ferma.

Credenziali e dati personali.

  • Il token di Cloudflare si mette in wp-config.php oppure si salva cifrato, e non viene mai mostrato né restituito.
  • I messaggi dei form sono visibili solo agli amministratori, si conservano per 180 giorni e si esportano o cancellano con gli strumenti privacy di WordPress.
  • I font si possono ospitare sul sito, così le pagine non chiedono i font ai server di Google.

Aggiornamenti firmati, nessuna licenza.

  • Nessuna chiave di licenza e nessuna telemetria.
  • Gli aggiornamenti sono spenti finché non li accendi, e si installano solo se firmati con la chiave di Histeon. Vedi Moduli e aggiornamenti.

Nessuna chiamata esterna che tu non abbia chiesto. Histeon si collega a servizi esterni solo per le funzioni che attivi tu:

FunzioneVerso doveQuando
AggiornamentiGitHubSolo se li accendi, al massimo ogni 12 ore
Font localiGoogle FontsSolo quando importi un font, una volta
Sito staticoCloudflareSolo se configuri la pubblicazione
Traduzione automatica (Lingua Bridge)Il servizio che scegli tuSolo con la tua chiave, su richiesta
Video YouTube o VimeoYouTube o VimeoQuando inserisci un video: titolo e immagine di anteprima, una volta
Sorgente JSON Da un indirizzoL'indirizzo che scrivi tuSolo se crei una sorgente di questo tipo
Invio dallo staging alla produzioneIl sito di produzione abbinatoSolo quando invii
Nomi proposti da un modello in Converti una pagina HTMLAnthropic, o un server compatibile che scegli tuSolo se imposti la tua chiave o il tuo server

Agenti IA con le briglie. Il connettore accetta solo un elenco chiuso di operazioni, ascolta solo sul tuo computer e non può pubblicare. Le abilità di WordPress lavorano con i permessi dell'utente, e un agente non pubblica da solo: la richiesta di pubblicare porta alla pagina Release, dove pubblica una persona.

Alla disinstallazione Histeon toglie le sue tabelle, opzioni, segreti e permessi. Restano i contenuti delle pagine.

Installazione blindata #

Ogni plugin di WordPress, moduli di Histeon compresi, gira nello stesso processo PHP con gli stessi permessi su database e file: un punto debole in uno qualsiasi riguarda tutto il sito. Per questo un modo efficace di ridurre i rischi non è dividere i plugin, ma togliere WordPress dalla strada dei visitatori. Se il sito lo permette, conviene questa configurazione.

  1. Sito pubblico statico. Pubblica la copia statica su Cloudflare oppure servila con Static Bridge (vedi Il sito statico). Chi visita riceve solo HTML e file: non esegue PHP e non tocca il database. Restano dinamici solo i filtri dei loop e i form, se li usi.
  2. Amministrazione privata. Rendi raggiungibili wp-admin e wp-login.php solo da chi lavora al sito: una regola del server per indirizzi IP fidati, una VPN, oppure un servizio di accesso come Cloudflare Access davanti all'indirizzo di WordPress. Con la pubblicazione su Cloudflare, WordPress può stare su un indirizzo che il pubblico non conosce.
  3. Solo i moduli che usi. Spegni in Histeon → Moduli quelli che non servono: un modulo spento non registra pagine, rotte né operazioni. Per esserne certi anche se qualcuno riaccende l'interruttore, scrivilo in wp-config.php:
define( 'XB_DISABLE_MODULES', 'lingua-bridge,static-bridge' );
  1. Credenziali fuori dal database. Metti il token di Cloudflare in wp-config.php (XB_CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN) invece che nelle impostazioni.
  2. Niente modifiche ai file da wp-admin. Con define( 'DISALLOW_FILE_EDIT', true ); in wp-config.php nessuno può modificare il codice di temi e plugin dall'amministrazione.
  3. Pochi utenti con i permessi forti. Dai unfiltered_html e xb_publish solo a chi serve (vedi Chi può scrivere codice).
  4. Aggiornamenti firmati. Accendili e lascia che Histeon installi solo le versioni firmate (vedi Moduli e aggiornamenti).

Non serve Docker né un server dedicato: tutto questo si fa anche su un hosting normale, con il sito statico su Cloudflare. Chi ospita in proprio può fare un passo in più e tenere WordPress su una rete interna.

L'interfaccia in italiano e in inglese

Histeon è scritto in italiano ed è disponibile anche in inglese. Questo capitolo spiega come sceglie la lingua in cui ti parla, e cosa resta fuori da questa scelta.

Qui si parla della lingua dell'interfaccia: menu, pulsanti, messaggi. Per pubblicare il sito in più lingue serve Lingua Bridge: vedi Siti in più lingue.

Come si sceglie la lingua #

Histeon usa la stessa lingua di WordPress per te:

  1. la lingua del tuo profilo (Utenti → Profilo → Lingua);
  2. se il profilo non ne sceglie una, la lingua del sito (Impostazioni → Generali → Lingua del sito).

Poi la regola è semplice:

La tua lingua in WordPressHisteon ti parla in
ItalianoItaliano
IngleseInglese
Qualsiasi altra (tedesco, francese, spagnolo…)Inglese

Chi usa WordPress in tedesco o in francese legge quindi l'inglese, non l'italiano: è la lingua più probabile da capire. Se il sito ha una traduzione di Histeon proprio per la tua lingua, Histeon usa quella.

Cambiare lingua #

Ogni persona può avere la sua lingua, sullo stesso sito:

  1. Apri Utenti → Profilo.
  2. In Lingua scegli quella che preferisci.
  3. Salva il profilo e ricarica il builder.

Un collaboratore straniero può così lavorare in inglese mentre tu continui in italiano. La scelta non cambia nulla del sito pubblico.

Dove vale #

La stessa regola vale per tutte le parti di Histeon, ognuna con le sue traduzioni:

  • Histeon: le schermate di amministrazione, il builder e le schede di Histeon nell'editor a blocchi;
  • Histeon CSS, il framework e il suo pannello;
  • Lingua Bridge, il modulo delle lingue;
  • Static Bridge, il modulo del sito statico;
  • Fondamenta, il tema di base, e XB Blank, il tema di compatibilità.

Valgono sia quando li usi come moduli di Histeon, sia come plugin separati.

Cosa non cambia con la lingua #

Alcune cose restano uguali in tutte le lingue, perché non sono testi dell'interfaccia:

  • I nomi tecnici: classi CSS, variabili, nomi dei permessi (xb_build, xb_publish), la sintassi delle espressioni e del codice.
  • I nomi dei prodotti: Histeon, Histeon CSS, Lingua Bridge, Static Bridge.
  • Le istruzioni per gli agenti IA: il manuale e le istruzioni che il connettore passa all'agente restano quelle originali.
  • Il contenuto del sito: i testi delle tue pagine, e anche i testi già pronti dei temi Fondamenta e XB Blank (come «Pagina non trovata»), sono contenuto, non interfaccia. Li traduce chi gestisce il sito, anche con Lingua Bridge.

Le parole in inglese #

Se lavori con colleghi che usano l'interfaccia in inglese, ecco come si chiamano le cose principali:

ItalianoInglese
sviluppo, in sviluppodevelopment, in development

pubblicare, releasepublish, release

componentecomponent
telacanvas
foglio di stilestylesheet

media query con nomenamed media query
query dei looploop query

Le scorciatoie da tastiera non cambiano con la lingua dell'interfaccia: Ctrl+K apre i comandi sia in italiano sia in inglese. Vedi Scorciatoie da tastiera.

Scorciatoie da tastiera

Nel builder quasi tutto si fa anche dalla tastiera. Questo capitolo elenca le scorciatoie di serie, i tasti che valgono in punti particolari (la tela, la struttura, il codice) e spiega come cambiarle.

Su Mac, al posto di Ctrl si usa ⌘ e al posto di Alt si usa ⌥. Nelle tabelle trovi la versione per Windows e Linux.

La palette dei comandi #

La scorciatoia più utile è Ctrl+K: apre la palette dei comandi, da cui si raggiunge tutto il builder. Scrivi qualche lettera, scegli con ↑ e ↓, conferma con Invio. Esc la chiude. La apre anche il pulsante Comandi nella barra in alto.

Accanto a ogni comando la palette mostra la sua scorciatoia, se ne ha una. Così le impari mentre lavori.

Le scorciatoie di serie #

ComandoWindows e LinuxMac
Apri i comandiCtrl+K⌘+K
Salva in sviluppoCtrl+S⌘+S
Aggiungi un elementoCtrl+I⌘+I
AnnullaCtrl+Z⌘+Z
RipetiCtrl+Shift+Z oppure Ctrl+Y⌘+Shift+Z oppure ⌘+Y
Duplica l'elemento selezionatoCtrl+D⌘+D
Elimina l'elemento selezionatoCancCanc
Sposta suAlt+↑⌥+↑
Sposta giùAlt+↓⌥+↓
Porta fuori dal contenitoreAlt+←⌥+←
Metti dentro l'elemento primaAlt+→⌥+→
Taglia l'elemento selezionatoCtrl+X⌘+X

Esc è fisso e non si può cambiare: chiude solo il riquadro aperto (Storia, Altro, Connettore AI), altrimenti la sezione aperta, poi il pannello del framework, poi toglie la selezione.

Mentre scrivi, le scorciatoie non ti rubano i tasti. Nei campi di testo e negli editor di codice funzionano solo «Apri i comandi» e «Salva in sviluppo». «Aggiungi un elemento» funziona anche nei campi, ma non nel codice. Tutte le altre valgono solo fuori da campi ed editor.

Le scorciatoie funzionano anche dopo un clic dentro la tela: l'anteprima passa i tasti al builder.

Copiare e incollare elementi #

Fuori dai campi di testo e dal codice:

TastiCosa fa
Ctrl+CCopia l'elemento selezionato con gli stili delle sue classi
Ctrl+VIncolla: un elemento copiato da Histeon (anche da un altro sito) apre la finestra per incollarlo; l'HTML copiato da altrove viene ripulito e inserito come elementi

Tasti in punti particolari #

Questi tasti valgono solo in un punto preciso del builder e non si possono cambiare.

Testo scritto nella tela (doppio clic su un titolo, un paragrafo, un link o un pulsante):

TastiCosa fa
InvioConferma il testo (anche un clic fuori)
Shift+InvioVa a capo nello stesso elemento
EscRimette il testo com'era
Ctrl+B / Ctrl+IGrassetto / corsivo
Ctrl+SConferma e salva in sviluppo

Pannello Struttura:

TastiCosa fa
F2Rinomina l'elemento (anche con un doppio clic sul nome)
Shift+F10 o il tasto menuApre il menu contestuale dell'elemento

Menu contestuale, palette e menu Aggiungi: ↑ e ↓ per muoversi (nel menu Aggiungi anche ← e →), Invio per scegliere, Esc per chiudere. Nel menu contestuale Inizio e Fine vanno alla prima e all'ultima voce.

Pannello HTML e codice:

TastiCosa fa
TabEspande un'abbreviazione Emmet (per esempio ul>li*3), oppure fa rientrare la riga
Esc, poi TabEsce dall'editor di codice con la tastiera (il suggerimento compare in basso a destra quando l'editor ha il focus)
F12 o Ctrl+clic su una classe o un idSeleziona l'elemento e apre il pannello CSS su quel selettore

Maniglie della larghezza della tela (ai lati dell'anteprima, dopo averle raggiunte con Tab):

TastiCosa fa
← / →Stringe o allarga di 10 pixel
Shift+← / Shift+→Stringe o allarga di 100 pixel
InizioLarghezza minima
Fine o EscTorna alla larghezza piena (anche con un doppio clic sulla maniglia)

Anteprima: Esc torna al builder.

Editor sul sito di Lingua Bridge: Ctrl+Invio salva la traduzione del testo e passa al successivo.

Cambiare le scorciatoie #

Ogni comando della palette può avere fino a tre combinazioni: azioni, sezioni, pannelli, snippet, componenti. Restano fuori solo le voci «Seleziona», che cambiano da una pagina all'altra.

  1. Apri la palette con Ctrl+K e scegli Scorciatoie da tastiera….
  2. Cerca il comando per nome, per gruppo o per combinazione.
  3. Premi + accanto al comando. Compare «Premi la combinazione… (Esc annulla)».
  4. Premi la combinazione che vuoi. Viene salvata subito.

Per togliere una combinazione premi ✕ accanto a lei. Per tornare alle scorciatoie di serie di un comando premi ↺; per tornare a quelle di serie di tutto il builder, Ripristina tutte.

Le tue scorciatoie si salvano nel tuo profilo: valgono per te, su ogni browser e computer, e non cambiano quelle dei colleghi.

Le regole delle combinazioni #

  • Forma: Ctrl, Alt e Shift (in quest'ordine, quelli che vuoi) più un tasto. Il tasto può essere una lettera, una cifra, da F1 a F12, una freccia, Canc, Inizio, Fine, Pag↑, Pag↓ oppure uno di , . / ; [ ] = -.
  • Una combinazione, un comando. Se è già usata, Histeon te lo dice con il nome del comando che la usa («… è già di …»): togli prima quella.
  • Combinazioni riservate, che restano al browser o alla scrittura e non si possono assegnare: Ctrl+C, Ctrl+V, Ctrl+X, Ctrl+A, Ctrl+W, Ctrl+T, Ctrl+N, Ctrl+Q, Ctrl+R, Ctrl+L, Ctrl+F, Ctrl+P, Ctrl+Shift+T, Ctrl+Shift+N, Ctrl+Shift+W e Backspace.
  • Il tasto fisico. Lettere, cifre e punteggiatura si riconoscono dalla posizione del tasto, non dal carattere che produce: per questo Alt (o ⌥ su Mac) non le cambia.

Con una tastiera dalla disposizione molto diversa (per esempio AZERTY), se una scorciatoia non risponde come ti aspetti, registrala di nuovo premendola: Histeon salva il tasto che premi davvero.

Una scorciatoia esegue il comando esattamente come la palette, con gli stessi controlli: non permette nulla che il comando non permetta già.

Problemi e domande frequenti

Le situazioni che capitano più spesso, con la causa e cosa fare. Se un problema non è qui, guarda il capitolo dell'argomento: spesso la risposta è nelle note.

Il builder e la pubblicazione #

Il builder non si apre, o non vedo «Modifica con Histeon» #

Compare il messaggio «Non hai i permessi per modificare questo contenuto con Histeon», oppure il link manca dall'elenco delle pagine.

Per aprire una pagina nel builder servono due cose: il permesso xb_build di Histeon e il permesso di WordPress di modificare quella pagina. Di serie xb_build ce l'hanno solo gli amministratori. Per i template serve anche il permesso di modificare il tema.

Cosa fare: chiedi a chi amministra il sito di darti xb_build (vedi Permessi e sicurezza). Il builder si apre su pagine, articoli, componenti e template: non su altri tipi di contenuto.

Ho salvato, ma sul sito non cambia nulla #

È il comportamento normale. Salva (o Ctrl+S) mette le modifiche in sviluppo: le vedi nel builder e in Anteprima, ma chi visita il sito vede ancora la versione online. Per metterle online usa Pubblica… nella barra in alto.

Se il problema resta dopo la pubblicazione, controlla:

  • la pagina in WordPress: se è ancora una bozza, i visitatori non la vedono;
  • i componenti tradotti: una traduzione di un componente si usa solo quando è pubblicata (vedi Siti in più lingue);
  • la copia statica: se il sito è servito come copia statica, vedi più avanti;
  • la cache del browser o del sito: prova a ricaricare la pagina forzando l'aggiornamento.

Non vedo il pulsante «Pubblica…» #

Il pulsante compare solo a chi ha il permesso xb_publish. Senza, puoi costruire e salvare in sviluppo, e qualcun altro pubblica: la schermata Release lo ricorda.

Se il pulsante c'è ma è disattivato, non ci sono modifiche da pubblicare. Se nella finestra di pubblicazione il pulsante Pubblica resta grigio, i controlli delle pagine hanno trovato errori: correggili, poi riprova.

Il pannello HTML è in sola lettura #

Succede quando un elemento contiene contenuto scritto fuori da Histeon (per esempio dall'editor a blocchi o da un'importazione) che il codice di Histeon non può rappresentare fedelmente. È una protezione: modificarlo potrebbe salvare qualcosa di diverso da quello che vedi. Il messaggio nel pannello lo spiega. Correggi l'elemento dalla struttura o dagli altri pannelli.

Il pannello JavaScript è in sola lettura #

Solo chi ha il permesso WordPress unfiltered_html può scrivere JavaScript. È voluto: vedi Chi può scrivere codice.

Ho pubblicato una versione sbagliata #

Apri Histeon → Release, trova la release giusta nello Storico delle release e premi Ripristina. La release ripristinata torna online come una release nuova, e le modifiche che hai in sviluppo restano dove sono. Vedi Versioni e release.

Il sito statico #

La copia statica non è aggiornata #

La copia statica è una fotografia del sito: cambia solo quando viene rigenerata (vedi Sito statico).

  • Con Static Bridge: in Histeon → Release, sezione Sito statico, controlla lo stato. Se dice «si aggiorna da sola a ogni release Histeon», dopo una pubblicazione Static Bridge aggiorna le pagine coinvolte entro poco tempo (le vedi come «pagine in coda di rigenerazione»). Se dice «aggiornamento automatico spento: rigenera da Static Bridge», rigenera a mano da Apri Static Bridge.
  • Con Cloudflare: se la pubblicazione automatica è attiva, ogni release pianifica un deploy in background, che parte poco dopo con le operazioni pianificate di WordPress. Altrimenti, nel builder apri la sezione Versioni e premi Pubblica ora su Cloudflare.
  • Stringhe tradotte: una stringa di Lingua Bridge vale per tutte le pagine di una lingua. Dopo averne cambiata una, rigenera la copia statica completa.

Il deploy o lo zip si fermano con un errore di contenuti privati #

Prima di pubblicare su Cloudflare o di creare lo zip, Histeon controlla ogni pagina. Se trova dati che non devono uscire (percorsi del server, codici di sicurezza, link di anteprima), si ferma. Di solito la causa è un plugin che stampa dati di sessione nelle pagine anche per i visitatori anonimi. Trova la pagina indicata e correggi la causa: il controllo non si può saltare.

Static Bridge non si accende #

Static Bridge richiede PHP 8.4. Con un PHP più vecchio la schermata Moduli mostra «Richiede PHP 8.4». Se invece il PHP va bene, i motivi del rifiuto compaiono nella stessa schermata (memoria, funzioni dei file, versione di WordPress).

Moduli e aggiornamenti #

Un modulo si è spento da solo #

Compare l'avviso «Histeon ha spento un modulo dopo un errore fatale». Histeon ha fermato il modulo perché causava un errore, e il resto del sito funziona. Aggiorna Histeon (o correggi la causa indicata nell'avviso), poi riaccendi il modulo in Histeon → Moduli. Vedi Quando un modulo va in errore.

L'amministrazione non si apre più #

Se sospetti un modulo, chi ha accesso ai file del sito può aggiungere a wp-config.php la riga:

define( 'XB_SAFE_MODE', true );

Nessun modulo verrà caricato. Quando il problema è risolto, togli la riga.

Non posso accendere Lingua Bridge (o Static Bridge) #

Se il messaggio dice «È attivo come plugin separato», lo stesso software è già attivo come plugin: va bene così, oppure disattiva il plugin e accendi il modulo. I dati sono gli stessi.

Se la schermata dice «Spento da wp-config.php», qualcuno l'ha spento con XB_SAFE_MODE o XB_DISABLE_MODULES: va tolta quella riga.

Histeon non trova aggiornamenti #

Sotto le impostazioni degli aggiornamenti compare «Nessuna release firmata trovata (o GitHub non raggiungibile)». Controlla che il repository sia scritto bene e che sia pubblico: un repository privato non risponde. Histeon riprova da solo ogni 12 ore.

Un aggiornamento è stato rifiutato #

Histeon installa solo pacchetti firmati con la chiave pubblica configurata. Se la firma non corrisponde, l'aggiornamento si ferma e il file scaricato viene cancellato. Controlla che la chiave nelle impostazioni sia quella di Histeon (la trovi in Moduli e aggiornamenti); se lo è, non forzare l'installazione a mano.

Importazioni #

L'importazione di una release o di un sito è rifiutata #

Il messaggio dice per esempio «File di release non valido: contenuto alterato o incompleto (hash)», oppure «File del sito non valido: …».

Ogni file esportato da Histeon contiene un'impronta del suo contenuto. Se il file è stato modificato dopo l'esportazione, anche di una virgola, oppure il download si è interrotto, l'impronta non torna e Histeon rifiuta tutto il file. Esportalo di nuovo dal sito di origine e non aprirlo né modificarlo prima di importarlo.

Altri motivi di rifiuto: il file supera i limiti di dimensione, oppure supera il limite di caricamento del server («File non ricevuto (forse supera il limite di caricamento del server)»).

Dopo il trasloco mancano dei contenuti #

Se il sito di origine ha tipi di contenuto creati in Histeon, al primo passaggio i loro contenuti vengono saltati con un avviso: WordPress li riconosce solo dopo che le impostazioni sono pubblicate. Pubblica la release, poi importa di nuovo lo stesso file: la seconda importazione non duplica nulla.

Un file XLIFF tradotto non si importa #

L'originale è cambiato dopo l'esportazione. Esporta di nuovo: il programma di traduzione ritroverà le frasi già tradotte nella sua memoria.

Ho importato un sito Etch e manca del JavaScript #

Histeon importa il JavaScript solo dai contenuti scritti da persone che avevano il permesso di scrivere codice (unfiltered_html). Il rapporto di importazione lo indica nella colonna Fiducia. Se lo script serve davvero, chi ha quel permesso lo riscrive nel builder. Vedi Importare un sito Etch.

Ho spento Etch: il sito cambierà? #

No. Con Etch spento, Histeon disegna da sé i contenuti salvati con Etch e il sito resta com'era. L'importazione resta consigliata, perché rende tutto modificabile nel builder.

Ho spento (o tolto) Automatic.css #

Finché i fogli che Automatic.css aveva generato restano nella cartella dei caricamenti del sito, restano caricati finché non importi: classi e variabili continuano a funzionare. Un avviso ricorda che l'importazione in Histeon CSS è consigliata. Non cancellare quei fogli prima di aver importato: senza di loro le pagine perderebbero i loro stili.

Lingue e agenti IA #

Il selettore di lingua non compare #

Compare solo quando la pagina ha almeno due versioni pubblicate. Le traduzioni in bozza non contano.

La traduzione fatta dall'IA non è online #

È voluto: agenti, traduzione automatica e XLIFF creano solo bozze, mai pagine pubblicate. Rivedi la bozza e pubblicala tu.

Il connettore IA non si collega #

Controlla, in ordine:

  1. che sul computer ci sia Node 18 o successivo;
  2. che il builder sia aperto in HTTPS oppure su localhost;
  3. che il connettore sia avviato con il comando copiato dopo l'ultima rigenerazione del segreto: se hai rigenerato il segreto, ricopia le istruzioni e riavvia il connettore;
  4. di aver premuto Collega questa scheda nel pannello ✦.

Ricaricando la scheda il collegamento si riapre da solo; chiudendola si chiude.

Domande frequenti #

Serve una licenza? No. Histeon è libero (GPL), senza chiavi di licenza. Anche Histeon CSS, Lingua Bridge e Static Bridge lo sono.

Histeon invia dati a qualcuno? Non da solo: nessuna telemetria. Si collega a servizi esterni solo per le funzioni che attivi tu (per esempio aggiornamenti, import dei font, Cloudflare, traduzione automatica con la tua chiave), e un agente IA che colleghi riceve ciò che legge dal sito. Vedi Permessi e sicurezza.

Un agente IA può rovinare il sito online? Dal connettore no: lavora solo in sviluppo e non può pubblicare. Nemmeno con le abilità di WordPress: può chiedere di pubblicare, ma a pubblicare, dalla pagina Release, è una persona.

Posso usare Histeon in inglese? Sì: scegli l'inglese nel tuo profilo WordPress. Vedi L'interfaccia in italiano e in inglese.

Posso cambiare le scorciatoie? Sì, ogni comando della palette: Ctrl+K → Scorciatoie da tastiera…. Vedi Scorciatoie da tastiera.

Spegnere un modulo cancella i dati? No, mai.

Introduction

Welcome to Histeon. This chapter explains what it is, who it is for, what sets it apart from other builders and what it needs to run.

What Histeon is #

Histeon is a visual builder for WordPress made for people who love clean code. You build pages by watching them grow on a canvas, but underneath there is always real HTML, with the tags and classes you chose, and CSS written per selector, nested as in SCSS.

The name comes from the Greek histós (ἱστός): the upright loom of ancient Greece, and also the cloth woven on it. A woven fabric, in other words: the web. In the logo, two uprights and a coral weft form an H.

Histeon saves pages in WordPress's standard format, the Gutenberg block format. Gutenberg is the format your content is stored in, not the interface you work in: that is why revisions, export, the REST API and SEO plugins keep working as always.

With Histeon you can:

  • write and edit the structure in the HTML, CSS and JavaScript panels, or with the mouse on the canvas and in the Structure;
  • create reusable components, with properties and slots;
  • show dynamic content with loops, conditions and visual queries;
  • work in development and take your changes live with a release, which can be restored;
  • choose your CSS framework, starting with Histeon CSS, the free framework included.

Who it is for #

Histeon is made for people who build websites for a living:

  • designers and front-end developers, who want full control over markup and CSS without giving up a visual interface;
  • agencies, who need reusable components, versions, previews to send to the client and controlled publishing;
  • editors and clients, who after handover edit the text directly in the WordPress block editor, with the page looking just as it does on the site.

You don't need to know how to program in PHP. You do need to know a little HTML and CSS: Histeon helps you write them, it doesn't hide them.

What sets it apart #

Clean code #

What you write is what ends up on the page: no hidden wrappers, no automatically generated classes. Styles are tied to the selector (a class or an id) and a page gets only the CSS it actually uses, both for your styles and for the framework's. The result is lightweight pages and code anyone can read.

Versions and releases #

Every save in the builder goes to development: the public site doesn't change. When you are ready, Publish… takes the chosen changes live as a release with a version number and notes. Before that you can look at the differences, make a visual comparison between the live page and the development one, and send the client a signed preview link that expires on its own. If something goes wrong, you restore the previous release.

Security #

  • All content is filtered with the permissions of the person who saves it, as in WordPress: someone who cannot write unfiltered HTML cannot insert scripts.
  • Untrusted content does not become trusted just because an administrator saves or publishes it.
  • JavaScript can be written and published only with WordPress's unfiltered HTML permission.
  • The builder canvas does not run the page's scripts.
  • Updates are signed and verified before installation.

Open source, no licenses #

Histeon is free software, licensed GPL-3.0-or-later. There are no license keys to enter, no “pro” versions to unlock, no telemetry and no mandatory external services. Updates come from the public releases on GitHub: the site asks which release is the latest and downloads its package and signature, without sending content, users or settings.

The Histeon family #

Histeon is a single plugin, with some parts you can turn on and off (the modules). Some also exist as separate plugins.

NameWhat it doesHow it is used
HisteonThe builder: canvas, structure, panels, components, versions and releasesMain plugin
Histeon CSSThe free CSS framework and design system: colors, fluid type and spacing scales, utility classes, recipes, popup panelModule included and turned on; also available as a standalone plugin for sites without the builder
Lingua BridgeMultilingual: translations as normal WordPress pages, translation memory, updating of changed sentencesModule included, off until you turn it on; also available as a separate plugin
Static BridgeThe static copy of the site: export, publishing to Cloudflare, forms and languagesModule included, off until you turn it on; requires PHP 8.4
FondamentaThe base block theme, with no Gutenberg presets or visual controls, with templates built with Histeon; it takes the place of XB BlankTheme installed separately (optional)

A module that is active as a separate plugin is not loaded twice: Histeon recognizes it and lets the plugin do the work. Turning off a module never deletes its data.

Requirements #

WhatRequirement
WordPress6.9 or later
PHP8.3 or later
Static Bridge (module)PHP 8.4 or later
Histeon CSS as a standalone pluginWordPress 6.6 and PHP 8.3 or later
DatabaseMySQL or MariaDB, like WordPress
ThemeAny; for site templates you need a block theme, such as Fondamenta

Histeon also works with WordPress on SQLite, through the official SQLite Database Integration plugin: it is handy for working locally with a single-file database, or for a site published as a static copy. WordPress officially supports only MySQL and MariaDB, so for a dynamic production site they remain the recommended choice.

On activation, administrators receive the permissions to build with Histeon and to publish releases.

How to read this manual #

The manual follows the path of someone building a site. If this is your first time, read the first chapters in order:

  1. Installation: the packages, signature verification, first run and modules.
  2. The builder: how the interface is laid out, the canvas, preview, saving and publishing.
  3. Elements: inserting, moving, writing text, the HTML panel and properties.
  4. Styles: classes, selectors, Mini GUI, media queries and the CSS that reaches your pages.
  5. Histeon CSS: the framework and the design system.
  6. Components: creating them, giving them props and slots, using and publishing them.

Read the other chapters when you need them: content and data, templates, versions and releases, static site, multilingual, importing from Etch, permissions and security. The contents on the left lists them all, and the search at the top finds a word anywhere in the manual.

A few conventions:

  • Interface labels are in bold, exactly as you see them on screen: Publish…, Save, Styles and selectors.
  • Paths in the WordPress menu use an arrow: Histeon → Settings.
  • Keys are written like this: Ctrl+S. On a Mac, use ⌘ instead of Ctrl.
  • Code to type is in a monospaced font: class="card".

Boxes like this one contain tips and warnings: they are worth reading.

Installation

This chapter takes you from the downloaded package to a Histeon that is ready to use: file verification, installation, first run, theme, modules and updates.

The packages #

Every Histeon release is made of a few files:

FileWhat it is
xb-<version>-<commit>.zipThe Histeon plugin, with the included modules (Histeon CSS, Lingua Bridge, Static Bridge)
fondamenta-<version>-<commit>.zipThe Fondamenta block theme (optional, recommended)
xb-blank-<version>-<commit>.zipThe XB Blank theme, only for sites that already use it
histos-css-<version>-<commit>.zipHisteon CSS as a standalone plugin, only for sites that don't use the builder
SHA256SUMSThe SHA-256 fingerprints of the packages
….zip.minisigThe signature of each package

The plugin's name in the files is xb: it is Histeon's technical prefix, kept for compatibility with existing sites.

If you use Histeon, don't also install the histos-css package: Histeon CSS is already inside Histeon. If you install it anyway, it stays idle and tells you so.

Verifying the packages #

Before uploading a package to a site, it is worth checking that it really is the one that was published. It takes two minutes.

The SHA-256 fingerprint #

Put the packages and SHA256SUMS in the same folder, then from a terminal:

SystemCommand
Linuxsha256sum -c SHA256SUMS
macOSshasum -a 256 -c SHA256SUMS
Windows (PowerShell)Get-FileHash .\xb-0.1.0-….zip -Algorithm SHA256

On Linux and macOS every file must show OK. On Windows, compare the fingerprint shown with the one written in SHA256SUMS by eye.

The signature #

The fingerprint tells you the file wasn't damaged during the download; the signature tells you it was produced by whoever holds the Histeon release key. Signatures use the standard minisign format, a small free program available for every system.

The Histeon public release key (id 7245FEACC0E94D12) is:

RWQSTenArP5FclHgI0qEErGDYUEQ6gL4cEhpgec49VKtqKfgl2TIAn5W

With the package and its .minisig in the same folder:

minisign -Vm xb-0.1.0-….zip -P RWQSTenArP5FclHgI0qEErGDYUEQ6gL4cEhpgec49VKtqKfgl2TIAn5W

If the signature is valid, minisign replies Signature and comment signature verified. Any other message means the file must not be installed.

It is the same key you enter in the update settings (later in this chapter): from then on Histeon verifies every update by itself.

Installing and activating #

  1. Check the requirements: WordPress 6.9 or later, PHP 8.3 or later.
  2. In WordPress open Plugins → Add New Plugin → Upload Plugin, choose the Histeon ZIP and install it.
  3. Activate the plugin.
  4. If you want the Fondamenta theme, upload it from Appearance → Themes → Add New Theme → Upload Theme and activate it.
  5. Follow the Histeon → First run screen (see the next section).
  6. Open a page with Edit with Histeon.

On activation, Histeon prepares its tables for versions and gives administrators the permissions to build and publish. The Histeon item appears in the WordPress menu, with Overview, Pages, Posts, Components, Templates, Releases, Versions, Modules and Settings.

The first run #

Until you have completed or skipped the first run, an admin notice takes you to Histeon → First run. It is a single page, with three choices.

  1. The CSS framework. These are the variables and classes the builder suggests to you. Histeon CSS is recommended and included. Core Framework and Automatic.css can be chosen if they are installed on the site; otherwise they show as “not installed”.
  2. The modules. Lingua Bridge and Static Bridge, to turn on only if you need them. A module that is active as a separate plugin, or that requires a newer PHP, is shown but cannot be changed from here.
  3. Where you are coming from. If the site has Etch content or uses Automatic.css, the page takes you to where the import is prepared. No import starts from here: everything happens in development, with a preview, and the site keeps working even without importing.

Then choose:

  • Save and start: applies the chosen framework and modules;
  • Skip for now: changes nothing.

All choices can be changed at any time. The first run remains reachable from Histeon → Settings → First run.

Until you choose a framework, Histeon uses Histeon CSS. If one day the chosen framework is no longer installed, Histeon falls back to Histeon CSS instead of leaving the site without styles.

The Fondamenta theme #

Fondamenta is a deliberately empty block theme:

  • no color, font or spacing presets;
  • no Gutenberg visual controls for colors, borders and spacing, so styling stays in your classes;
  • templates for pages, posts, archives and the 404 page, with header and footer built with Histeon.

It isn't required: any theme will do. To edit the site templates from the builder (the Templates section), however, you need a block theme. Fondamenta takes the place of XB Blank: if the site still uses XB Blank, the chapter on templates explains the switch.

Modules #

Histeon → Modules shows a card for each module, with icon, status, description, version and requirements, the toggle and the link to its settings.

StatusWhat it means
ActiveThe module is on and working
OffThe module is included but not loaded
Active as a separate pluginThe same software is active from the Plugins page: the module stays idle and the plugin is managed from there
Requires PHP …The server's PHP version is not enough
Turned off after a fatal errorThe module caused an error and was stopped (see below)
Turned off by wp-config.phpA constant in wp-config.php keeps it off

To turn a module on or off, use the Turn on … or Turn off … button on its card. Turning off a module never deletes its data: languages, translations, static copies and settings stay where they are.

When a module that is turned on has pages of its own, its menus appear at the bottom of the Histeon menu.

Notes on the individual modules:

  • Histeon CSS is on by default. If you turn it off, only the base --xb-* tokens remain (components use them), while classes, palettes and the popup panel disappear.
  • Static Bridge requires PHP 8.4. Before turning on, it checks PHP, WordPress, memory and file access: if something is missing, nothing changes and the reasons appear on the screen.
  • Lingua Bridge and Static Bridge, if also installed as separate plugins, cannot be activated from the Plugins page while the module is on: the page shows “Active as a Histeon module”.

If a module fails #

If a module causes a fatal error, Histeon turns it off by itself: the next request starts without that module and the rest of the site works. Administrators see a notice with the module, the file and the line of the error, and the Open Histeon → Modules button. After fixing or updating the module, turn it back on.

If the admin area doesn't open at all, you can turn modules off from wp-config.php:

define( 'XB_SAFE_MODE', true );                               // no modules
define( 'XB_DISABLE_MODULES', 'lingua-bridge,static-bridge' ); // only these

When you remove the constant, the module goes back to the state you had chosen.

The Overview #

Histeon → Overview is the starting point after installation: it shows what is live, what is waiting to go live and where to pick up again. The Site status card gathers what deserves a look, with a green dot (all good) or a coral one (worth a look) and the link to where to fix it: modules, static copy, recommended imports.

Updates #

Histeon updates from the public releases on GitHub, like any plugin from the WordPress admin: no license, no telemetry. The site only asks which release is the latest and downloads its package and signature, without sending content, users or settings.

Automatic updates are off by default. To turn them on:

  1. Open Histeon → Settings → Updates.
  2. In Repository enter the GitHub repository of the releases, in the form owner/name.
  3. In Minisign public key paste the Histeon release key (the one from the “Verifying the packages” section).
  4. Save. With Check for updates on GitHub you can check right away; the screen shows the latest signed release found and the installed version.

Alternatively, you can set the same values in wp-config.php, where they take precedence over the settings:

define( 'XB_UPDATE_REPOSITORY', 'owner/name' );
define( 'XB_UPDATE_PUBLIC_KEY', 'RWQSTenArP5FclHgI0qEErGDYUEQ6gL4cEhpgec49VKtqKfgl2TIAn5W' );

How it works:

  • Histeon checks for releases at most every 12 hours. Only a published release (not a draft or pre-release) that contains the package and its signature counts.
  • If the version is newer than the installed one, WordPress shows it among the updates like any plugin.
  • Before installing, Histeon downloads the package and the signature and verifies them with the configured key. If something doesn't match, the update stops with the reason and the downloaded file is deleted.
  • The included modules (Lingua Bridge, Static Bridge) update together with Histeon, with the release that contains them. A separate plugin, on the other hand, keeps its own updates.

Updates only work if the repository's releases are public: a private repository doesn't answer the anonymous requests Histeon uses.

The builder

The builder is the Histeon workspace: a canvas with the real page, the structure of the elements and the code panels. This chapter gives you the full tour, from opening it to publishing.

Opening the builder #

You can open a page, a post, a component or a template in several ways:

  • from the list of pages or posts, with the Edit with Histeon action under the title;
  • from the admin bar, in the Histeon → Edit this page with Histeon menu, even while you are looking at the page on the site;
  • from the block editor, with the Edit with Histeon button in the top bar;
  • from Histeon → Pages, Posts, Components or Templates.

From the block editor, the builder opens full screen over Gutenberg. If there are unsaved changes in Gutenberg, Histeon asks you to save them first. The ← Gutenberg button at the top left closes the builder and takes you back to the editor: if a release has changed the page in the meantime, the editor reloads; otherwise a notice reminds you that there are changes saved in development, which will appear after publishing.

The interface #

The builder is divided into four areas:

AreaWhereWhat it contains
Top barAt the topDocument title, Add, Commands, Undo and Redo, Preview, Save, Publish…
Section columnOn the leftIcons for the site sections, which open over the canvas
CanvasIn the centerThe page as visitors will see it, at the width you choose
PanelsOn the right and at the bottomStructure and Properties on the right; HTML, CSS and JavaScript at the bottom

The bottom panels show the code of the selected element, not of the whole page: that way you see and edit only what you are working on.

The top bar #

ItemWhat it does
Histeon logoOpens the Histeon Overview in the admin
← GutenbergGoes back to the block editor
AddOpens the menu to insert an element (Ctrl+I)
TitleThe open document; for a template or a component the type appears too
LanguagesWith Lingua Bridge turned on, the page's languages: a click opens the translation in the builder
StatusMessages such as “Unsaved changes” or the outcome of the last save
CommandsOpens the command palette (Ctrl+K)
Undo, Redo, HistoryGo back and forward through your changes
New componentCreates an empty component and opens it
PreviewSaves and shows the real page over the builder
PublishedOpens the live page in a new tab
SaveSaves to development (Ctrl+S)
Publish…Takes the saved changes live (only for those with permission to publish)

The site sections #

The icons in the left column open the sections over the canvas; Close ✕ or a second click on the icon takes you back to the canvas.

SectionWhat it is for
ContentThe site's pages and posts, to move from one document to another without leaving
TemplatesThe templates and template parts of the block theme
Styles and selectorsAll the site's selectors, the global sheets and the named media queries
Data and queriesLoop queries and data sources
Loop filtersThe filters visitors use to narrow down a list
Types and fieldsContent types, taxonomies and fields
ComponentsThe site's components
VersionsReleases, with export, import and restore
Single fileThe page or component as a single file with HTML, CSS and JavaScript
ChecksAccessibility, SEO and performance of the page
Design systemThe framework variables and the Histeon CSS generator
CommentsComments from the review with the client

When you are working on a component, Component props appears too. At the bottom of the column you find the command palette, the button to show or hide the bottom panels and the theme button, which on each click goes from like the system (light by day, dark by night, if your computer switches by itself) to light and to dark. Histeon remembers only the choice you make. If the chosen framework is Core Framework or Automatic.css, a button opens its panel over the canvas: after saving something there, ↻ Apply to the preview updates variables, classes and preview.

Arranging the panels #

The panels adapt to the way you work:

  • drag a panel's header to move it to another area (left, right, bottom) or reorder it;
  • ▾ collapses it to just the header, ⤢ expands it to fill its area;
  • drag the edges of the areas to change their width and height, and the line between two panels of the same area (for example HTML and CSS) to give one or the other more room; from the keyboard, arrows, Home and End on the line;
  • the ⋯ menu of each panel offers the same moves from the keyboard, plus Restore the layout, which can be undone for a few seconds.

The layout stays saved in this browser, including the expanded panel.

On tablets and phones #

Below 1024 px wide the side columns become drawers over the canvas: the Side panels button in the left bar opens and closes them. In the top bar Save and Publish… always stay visible, the other actions move to the ⋯ menu. Below 768 px the builder warns that a wider screen is needed; you can carry on anyway. In content mode a phone is fine: the canvas takes the whole width and one tap on a text opens it for editing. With a finger or a pen you drag an element of the Structure by pressing its icon, and a panel by pressing its ⠿ grip; the tab of the selected element in the canvas drags as with the mouse.

The canvas #

The canvas shows the page with the site's real styles, but without running its scripts: it is a safe space to work in.

  • Selecting: a click on an element selects it; the Structure, the Properties and the code panels switch to it. Esc clears the selection.
  • Moving: above the selected element a tab with its name appears (for example ⠿ Card). Drag it: a coral line shows whether it will go before or after, a dashed box whether it will go inside. Esc cancels the drag.
  • Spacing: on the selected element, handles appear for padding (coral, on the inner sides) and for the top and bottom margin (indigo). When you drag them, the value snaps to the framework's spacing scale, for example var(--space-m); holding Alt gives whole pixel values. A label shows the value while you drag.

The spacing handles write to the CSS of the element's first class. If the element has no classes, the canvas writes nothing and the status line asks you to add one.

Canvas widths #

The preview has no fixed devices: the page is drawn at the width you choose, and the site's media queries respond to exactly that width.

  • Handles: on both sides of the canvas there are two handles to drag. They also work from the keyboard: arrows ±10 px, Shift+arrows ±100 px, Home for the minimum width, Esc or End to go back to fitting.
  • Px field: in the bar above the canvas, type a width between 240 and 3840 pixels.
  • Fit: brings the canvas back to the width of the available space.
  • Site breakpoints: next to the field, a button for each named media query (for example tablet 1024) sets the canvas to that width.

If you choose a width larger than the space, the page is scaled down to fit and the bar says “scaled to …%”. Media queries still see the real width. The chosen width stays in this browser.

The canvas that follows #

When you set the canvas to a width other than “Fit”, the style changes you make with the Mini GUI and the spacing handles automatically go into the media query for that width:

  • if one of the site's named media queries applies at that width, that one is used (for example @media (--mobile));
  • otherwise Histeon creates a query at the canvas width, for example @media (width <= 600px).

Above the canvas, a pill says where the changes go, for example Changes in @media (--mobile). A click turns it off and it becomes Changes for all widths; another click turns it back on. Fit goes back to writing for all widths. The chapter on styles explains how to choose state and width by hand.

The preview #

The Preview button saves to development and shows the page over the builder, full screen. It isn't the canvas drawing: it is the real page, rendered by WordPress with its scripts (sliders, menus, filters), in the development version.

  • Width: Full, Tablet (820 px) or Phone (390 px).
  • Link lasts: how long the link you send the client works: 1 hour, 1 day, 2 days (if you don't choose) or 7 days. Next to it, Expires on … gives the date and time.
  • Copy link: copies the link with the chosen duration, to send to the client.
  • New tab: opens the same preview outside the builder. It is a signed link that expires on its own and doesn't require a login: you can send it to the client.
  • Back to the builder or Esc: bring the builder back as it was.

There is no admin bar in the preview, because visitors don't see it. The preview of a template opens on the sample post chosen in the canvas bar (Preview with).

Undo, Redo and History #

Ctrl+Z undoes, Ctrl+Shift+Z or Ctrl+Y redo. Next to the two buttons, History opens the latest steps (up to 200) described in words, most recent first: “Added div.card”, “Moved …”, “CSS of .card”.

  • A click on a step takes the page back to before that step, in one go.
  • Undone steps stay grayed out above Now until you change something else: a click redoes them.
  • Esc or a click outside closes the window.

The history lives in the open tab and is lost when you reload the page. Saved versions stay in the Versions section and in Histeon → Versions.

Saving and publishing #

Histeon keeps work in progress and the public site separate.

Save (Ctrl+S) saves to development: the public site doesn't change. You can save as often as you like, look at the preview and send it to the client. While there are unsaved changes, the Save button carries a coral dot; notices in the status line (copied, pasted…) go away with the next edit. If you close the tab with unsaved changes, the browser warns you.

Publish… takes the saved changes live. The window shows:

  1. What goes live: each changed document (pages, components, styles, settings), with a checkbox to choose which ones to publish and Differences to see what changes compared with the published site.
  2. The checks of the pages that change. If blocking is turned on in the settings, publishing waits for them to finish without errors.
  3. Compare: optional, puts the live page and the development one side by side, on a large screen and on a phone, and marks what changes.
  4. Version and Notes: the release number (for example 1.2.0) and what changes.

A click on Publish takes everything live together. If something fails, nothing changes. Every release can be restored from the Versions section or from Histeon → Releases: all the details are in the Versions and releases chapter.

If an editor changed some text in Gutenberg while you were working in the builder, publishing keeps their text and your structure. If you both changed the same text, yours wins and the builder tells you.

The command palette #

Ctrl+K (or the Commands button) opens the palette: type a few letters and choose. You'll find actions (Save in development, Publish…, Duplicate the selected element), insertions (Insert: Section), the builder sections, the items to move panels and the page elements to select.

Search and replace #

From the palette, Search and replace in the page… changes a word or a phrase across the whole page. Choose where: in the Texts (never inside tags or {…} expressions), in the Classes (only a class with exactly that name: card does not touch card__title) and in the Link addresses; Match case tells capitals apart. Before replacing you see how many replacements there will be and a list of how it was and how it will be. All of them are undone with a single Ctrl+Z.

Shortcuts #

CommandKeys
Open commandsCtrl+K
Save in developmentCtrl+S
Add an elementCtrl+I
UndoCtrl+Z
RedoCtrl+Shift+Z, Ctrl+Y
Duplicate the selected elementCtrl+D
Delete the selected elementDelete
Move up / downAlt+↑ / Alt+↓

On a Mac use ⌘ instead of Ctrl. Esc closes only the open popover (History, More, AI connector) and puts the focus back on its button; if there is none, it closes the open section, then clears the selection. After Delete the next element stays selected (or the previous one, or the container), so you can delete several elements in a row.

Each command in the palette can have up to three key combinations: choose Keyboard shortcuts… in the palette, select a command and press the new combination. Combinations reserved by the browser (copy, paste, new tab…) can't be assigned. Your shortcuts apply only to you, in every browser, and each one can be restored, or all of them at once with Restore all.

Histeon inside Gutenberg #

Even without opening the builder, a selected Histeon element in the WordPress block editor has its own panels in the sidebar:

  • Histeon · Appearance. Spacing, text, colours and borders, layout and sizes, as buttons with the site's design values: people who don't write CSS change how it looks with a click. A second click on an active button removes it. When picks a state (on hover, with focus…) and On which screens one of the site's widths: the button writes only there. Some settings appear when they apply, for example columns only for a grid.
  • Histeon · Classes. The element's classes, to add or remove. Appearance is changed on the class: an element without classes asks for a name first.

Under the page, Histeon · Code shows the element's HTML, CSS and JavaScript. It's the same CSS the Appearance buttons write: after a click, the declaration it touched is selected in the code, and a note says what changed ("padding of .card: from … to …"). So you can always see what a button changes, and where.

Style changes show in the page right away and are saved with Save to development: they go live with the next release, like those made in the builder.

Components in Gutenberg #

A component shows as it does on the site. When it's selected, the sidebar shows its content (the props): texts, images from the media library, links with suggestions from the site's pages, short choices as buttons. Default value puts a content back to the component's own.

If the component has areas (slots), a box appears under it for each one, with its name: inside, you add and edit blocks as in a page, while the rest of the component stays as it is. Add content to “…” creates a missing area.

People with the Histeon client role see all this read-only, together with the code: they understand what would change and where, and changes are made by whoever manages the site.

The section library in Gutenberg #

In the editor's ⋮ menu, Histeon · Library opens the section library in the sidebar. Insert puts the section after the selected block, with its styles. If one of its classes already exists on the site with different styles, the section brings it under a new name (card becomes card-2, with its parts), so the rest of the site doesn't change; a notice says which.

Lists and conditions in Gutenberg #

Selecting a repeated list (a loop), Histeon · Repeat says in one sentence what it shows and lets you pick the query, how many items and how many to skip. Selecting a branch of a condition, Histeon · Condition writes it in words and composes it with three fields: what to check, the condition and the value. Below is how it's written in code, for those who prefer to write it by hand.

Locking a page's structure #

In the page settings, in Gutenberg, Histeon · Structure → Structure locked leaves whoever doesn't build with Histeon (an editor, for example) only the texts and pictures: no blocks added, removed or moved. The site checks it on save too. Builders can still change everything.

Quick commands in Gutenberg #

Ctrl+K (⌘K on a Mac) opens WordPress's command palette, which also holds Histeon's: type "Histeon" to open the page in the builder, see its versions, go to releases, insert a section from the library or save the styles to development.

The five-step guide #

The first time you open the builder, a short guide shows the five places a page is made in: the page itself, the structure, the style, the code, saving and publishing. Move with Next or the arrow keys, close it with Skip the guide or Esc, and it doesn't come back on its own. To see it again: command palette (Ctrl+K) → Five-step guide.

Elements

A Histeon page is made of real HTML elements: sections, headings, paragraphs, images, links. This chapter explains how to add them, organize them, write their text and edit their code and properties.

Inserting an element #

Open the insert menu in one of these ways:

  • the Add button in the top bar;
  • the + button in the header of the Structure;
  • Ctrl+I, even while typing in a field.

At the top of the menu there is the search (Search for an element or a component…) and, below it, the exact place where the new element will go: “Inside …, at the end” if the selected element is a container, otherwise “After …”, or “At the end of the page” if nothing is selected. The Before, Inside and After buttons next to it change the place. Choose with a click, or with the arrows and Enter.

GroupWhat it contains
ElementsSection, Container, Title, Paragraph, Link, Button, Image, List, Condition, Post loop, Slot (in components), Post content
InteractionsTabs, Accordion, Mobile menu, Language switcher, Reveal on scroll: accessible out of the box, without writing JavaScript
MediaImage from the library… and SVG (paste or file)…
ComponentsThe site's components
Site PHP blocksThe blocks that the site's theme or a plugin make available

Every inserted element is a step you can undo with Ctrl+Z.

The Structure #

The Structure panel shows the page as a tree: each row is an element, with its children indented below. A click selects the element (on the canvas too), the arrow next to it expands and collapses its children.

Each row helps you recognize the element without opening it:

  • the label, if you gave it one, or else the tag and the class;
  • the automatic BEM label: an element with no label or class, inside a block with a class, shows its BEM name; for example an h2 inside .card shows card__title;
  • the section's name: a section or a box takes the name of its first heading (for example “I giusti”), open or closed; without headings, its aria-label or its id;
  • the text, in quotes: a few words of the heading or of the first text it contains. For an image, its alternative text. Hovering over the row shows the full text;
  • what it is, after the text and smaller: “Paragraph”, “Heading H2”, “Card”…

Formatting inside a text (bold, italics, links, line breaks) is not a row of its own: a paragraph with a bold word and a line break stays one row, with its words. The arrow opens it if you want to reach the bold word.

At the top of the Structure, the Search the structure field finds elements by words of their text, tag, class, id (#chi-siamo), name or kind (“paragraph”): every word typed must match. Results have a coloured bar on the left and their branches open; Enter selects the next one, Shift+Enter the previous one, Esc clears the field.

Above the canvas, the path tells you where you are: for example “Page › I giusti › Body › Il giusto legge…”. A click on a step selects that element; “Page” clears the selection.

Several elements at once: Ctrl+click (or ⌘+click on a Mac), in the Structure or in the canvas, adds an element to the selection or takes it away; Shift+click selects every sibling between the selected one and the one clicked. The path above the canvas says how many more are selected. Duplicate, Delete, Move up/down and Wrap (from the context menu, the palette or their keys) act on all of them, in a single undo step; siblings next to each other go together into the same container. The Properties show the first selected element. A plain click goes back to one element.

To move an element, drag its row in the Structure: toward the top or bottom of a row it goes before or after, in the middle it goes inside, where allowed.

Moving, duplicating, deleting #

ActionHow
Move up or down among siblingsAlt+↑ / Alt+↓
Move out of the container, or into the element beforeAlt+← / Alt+→
Move anywhereBy dragging the row in the Structure or the tab on the canvas
DuplicateCtrl+D
DeleteDelete
Select the parentFrom the context menu

The same commands are also in the Properties panel, under In the structure: Up, Down, Duplicate, Delete.

The context menu #

Right-click a row in the Structure or an element in the canvas (or press the menu key, or Shift+F10). In the canvas the right click also selects the element under the pointer; holding Shift opens the browser's menu instead. You move through it with the arrows, and Esc closes it.

ItemWhat it does
Edit the componentOn a component: opens its definition in place
Write the text on the pageOn a text: opens it for editing on the canvas
CopyCopies the element with the styles of its classes
Paste herePastes inside or after the element
DuplicateLike Ctrl+D
Wrap in a div / a section / a linkThe element becomes the child of a new container
Remove the wrapperThe element disappears and its children take its place
Create component…Turns the element into a reusable component
BEM classes for childrenGives children without a class a block__part name
RenameChanges the label in the Structure (F2)
Copy HTMLCopies the element's code
Copy style / Paste styleTakes an element's classes and style attribute and gives them to another (replacing its own); undone with Ctrl+Z
Select the parent, Move up, Move down, DeleteLike the commands of the same name

Items that can't be used stay visible but disabled; hovering over them tells you why.

Writing text on the canvas #

Double-click a heading, a paragraph, a link or a button: the element opens for editing in place, with a coral border.

  • Enter or a click outside confirms; Esc puts the text back as it was.
  • Shift+Enter starts a new line.
  • Ctrl+B and Ctrl+I apply bold and italic.
  • Ctrl+S confirms and saves.

Pasted text always arrives as plain text. Only elements that contain only text, even formatted, can be edited here. Text with expressions (for example {this.title}), an element with other elements inside or an element repeated by a loop are edited in the HTML panel. If you write something in the canvas that can't stay there, the text isn't lost: it moves to the HTML panel, as you wrote it, to be finished there.

The HTML panel #

The HTML panel shows the code of the selected element; the Whole page button shows the entire page. What you write appears on the canvas right away.

  • Inside a class="…" attribute, the panel suggests the framework's classes.
  • If the code has an error, the panel points it out and the canvas shows the last valid version until you fix it. Wrong code counts as "not saved": Ctrl+S says what it didn't save, closing the tab makes the browser warn you, and before you select another element or undo, Histeon asks whether to drop it. The same goes for a global sheet rule with unbalanced braces.
  • Ctrl+click (⌘+click on a Mac) or F12 on a class or an id opens the CSS panel on that selector.

If an element contains content written outside Histeon that the code can't represent faithfully, the panel becomes read-only. Fix it from the Structure or the Properties.

Abbreviations (Emmet style) #

Type an abbreviation and press Tab: it becomes HTML, with the cursor in the first empty element.

TypeGet
section.hero>h2.hero__title{Hello}+pa section with a heading and a paragraph
.card#top<div id="top" class="card"></div>
a[href="/x" target=_blank]{Go}a link with its attributes
ul>.item$*3three li with the classes item1, item2, item3
div>(h3+p)*2^footerrepeated groups; ^ climbs up one level

A single word expands only if it is an HTML tag: Tab after any other word indents, as usual.

The HTML panel language #

The HTML panel uses normal HTML, with a few stricter rules and some additions for dynamic content.

HTML rules.

  • Every element is closed explicitly: <p>Text</p>, never <p> on its own. <div/> counts as <div></div>.
  • HTML tags are written in lowercase. A name with a capital initial is a component: <Card>.
  • A line break between two elements doesn't become a space. If you need a space between two links on different lines, write {" "} or keep them on the same line.
  • <!-- … --> comments are removed.

Expressions. Between curly braces you write a value to display: {this.title} is the title of the current page. They work in text and in attributes: <a href={post.url}>. To write a literal brace, use \{ and \}. The main values are this (the current content), site, user (the visitor), url and data (the data sources). A missing value doesn't break the page: it becomes empty. All the available values are described in the Content and data chapter.

You can transform a value with modifiers:

ExampleResult
{post.excerpt.truncateWords(20)}The first 20 words
{post.date.date("j F Y")}The date in the chosen format
{post.title.upper()}The title in uppercase
{post.excerpt ?? "No excerpt"}A fallback text if the value is missing

Conditions.

{#if this.image}
  <img src={this.image.url} alt={this.image.alt}>
{:else}
  <p>No image</p>
{/if}

Loops. They repeat a piece of the page for each result of a query:

{#loop posts(limit: 3) as post}
  <article>
    <h3><a href={post.url}>{post.title}</a></h3>
  </article>
{:else}
  <p>No posts yet.</p>
{/loop}

The part after {:else} shows when the query finds nothing. There, post does not exist.

Labels. The xb:label="Latest posts" attribute gives the element a name in the Structure. It doesn't reach the page.

Pasting #

Outside text fields and code editors:

  • Ctrl+C copies the selected element with the styles of the classes it uses;
  • Ctrl+V pastes it, even into another Histeon site.

When you paste from another site, Paste from … opens, showing what happens to each class:

  • new: it is added with its styles;
  • same as the site's: the one already there is used;
  • in conflict: choose whether to import with a new name (for example card-2) or use this site's.

Components the site doesn't have are flagged: they will stay empty.

You can also paste HTML from other sources, such as a web page or another builder: it becomes editable elements. Scripts, embedded styles, iframes, forms and javascript: addresses are removed first. Classes stay.

SVG #

Add → SVG (paste or file)… inserts an icon or a vector illustration as editable elements. Paste the code or choose an .svg file from your computer (up to 200 KB). Before insertion the SVG is cleaned of scripts, styles, animations and event attributes, and the window tells you what was removed. For colors that follow the text, use currentColor.

Add → Icon from the library… opens the roughly 1,700 Lucide icons (ISC licence) bundled with Histeon: no CDN, no icon font. Search them by name (house, arrow right, mail); a click inserts the icon as an inline SVG, with the class icon icon--name, the text colour (currentColor) and aria-hidden="true" (if the icon alone says something, give the button or link around it an accessible name).

Properties and attributes #

The Properties panel shows the selected element: the Tag, the Content if it is a text, and the fields needed for that type of element, named in plain words.

ElementFields
HeadingThe level, from h1 to h6, with one click
LinkAddress (also Choose a file from the library…, for a PDF or an image), Open in a new tab, tooltip
ImageChoose image… from the library, alternative text, width, height, loading
ButtonType (button, submit, reset), disabled
Form fieldType, name, placeholder text, required
VideoFile, poster, controls, autoplay, muted, loop
Embedded contentAddress, title, loading
Numbered listStart from, reversed

Each element also has its Identity: the id (useful for #anchor links) and the accessible name. Where something important is missing, such as an image's alternative text, the field tells you.

Below, the Attributes section lists all the attributes: edit the value, remove one with ✕ or add a new one with a name and value. Emptying a field removes the attribute.

What can be saved is always decided by the server, based on your permissions: for example, event attributes such as onclick are kept only for those who can write unfiltered HTML.

Quick actions #

  • BEM classes (in the Properties) or BEM classes for children (in the context menu): children without a class receive a block__part name, where the block is the element's class and the part comes from the child's label or role (title, text, image, link…).
  • Rename: double-click the row in the Structure or F2. The label is a line of text of at most 60 characters; an empty label goes back to the automatic one.
  • Create component…: turns the element and its content into a component. The Components chapter explains how.

Styles

In Histeon, styling is written in CSS, tied to a selector: a class or an id. The builder helps you with SCSS-style nesting, a bar of visual controls (the Mini GUI), named media queries and a selector manager. Each page gets only the CSS it actually uses.

Classes and selectors #

Histeon styles live in two places:

  • the selector CSS: one entry for each class or id you have styled, for example .card or #contacts. It is the right place for almost everything;
  • the global sheets: the CSS that applies to the whole site, such as a reset, the variables in :root or the styling of bare tags.

Styles apply across the whole site: the same .card looks the same on every page that uses it. Like content, they are saved in development and go live with the next release.

The CSS panel #

Select an element: at the top of the CSS panel a pill appears for each of its classes, one for its id (if it has one) and one for the Global sheet. Choose the pill and write.

In the panel you write only the body of the rule, without repeating the selector:

padding: var(--space-m);
border-radius: var(--radius-m);

If the element has no classes, the panel asks you to add one: type it in the field (for example hero) and press Add class. The field already proposes a name, from the nearest block with a class and from what the element is (for example hero__title for a heading inside .hero): with the field empty, Add class uses it.

A link, a bold word or a span inside a sentence is part of the text: you edit it by typing in the paragraph. To style one on its own, Alt+click it on the canvas: it becomes an element with its own class (for example hero__link), selected and ready for the CSS panel.

While you type, the editor helps you:

  • framework variables: typing --sp opens the list of variables; outside var() the completion inserts var(--space-m);
  • abbreviations at the start of a declaration: df (display: flex), dg (display: grid), jcc, aic, mxa, w100 and more;
  • recipes: @ followed by the name, for example @centra, expands into a group of ready-made declarations (see the Histeon CSS recipes);
  • named media queries: after @media ( the site's names are suggested.

While you write, the panel underlines what is wrong: unclosed braces, variables and named media queries the site doesn't have, !important, declarations without a value, fixed values equal to a token (with the token to use) and classes outside the BEM scheme. Hover over the underline to read why. The same warnings, for the page's selectors, are in Checks, CSS area.

Nesting as in SCSS #

Inside a rule you can nest states, parts, variants and media queries:

padding: var(--space-m);
&:hover { color: var(--primary); }
@media (--tablet) { padding: var(--space-l); }
&__title { font-weight: 700; &--big { font-size: 2em; } }
&--dark { background: #000; }

On .card this also produces .card__title, .card__title--big and .card--dark: rules that start with &__ or &-- become sibling rules with the concatenated name, following the BEM convention. Each one reaches a page only if the page uses that class.

The Mini GUI #

The Mini GUI is a compact bar above the CSS editor that writes the most common declarations for you, while you look at the element on the canvas. The Mini GUI button opens and closes it; the choice stays in this browser.

GroupProperties
Layoutdisplay, direction, wrap, grid columns (2, 3, 4, auto), alignment, gap
Spacingpadding, vertical and horizontal padding, margins
Sizewidth, maximum width, minimum height, aspect ratio, image fit
Textsize, weight, line height, alignment, balanced wrapping, color
Colors and bordersbackground, radius, shadow, border, opacity
Positionposition, offsets, layer, overflow, container type

How it works:

  • each button writes a real declaration into the selector's CSS, and the canvas shows it right away; a second click on the active value removes it;
  • a group shows only what has an effect at that moment: alignment only with flex or grid, offsets only with a position set. “+N properties not needed right now” shows the others;
  • spacing, text, radii, shadows and colors come from the active framework's scale, always as var(--…);
  • a coral dot marks the groups that already have values;
  • nested rules you wrote by hand stay as they are.

State and width #

Next to the Mini GUI, two menus choose where to write (● marks the items that already have declarations):

  • State: Normal, :hover, :focus-visible, :active, ::before, ::after;
  • Width: All, Same as canvas, the site's named media queries, or the current canvas width (for example ≤ 1104px).

The Mini GUI writes inside the right nested block, for example &:hover { … } or @media (--tablet) { &:hover { … } }, creates it if it is missing and removes it when it is left empty. While a state is chosen, the canvas shows the element in that state, as if the mouse were over it.

When you set the canvas to a certain width, the Mini GUI follows it automatically: this is the canvas that follows, described in the chapter on the builder. Choosing another width by hand stops following it; Same as canvas starts following it again.

More: width queries and quick actions #

The More ▾ button opens two groups of commands.

At the canvas width (for example “At 720px”):

  • @media up to here adds a @media (width <= 720px) { } block;
  • @media from here up adds @media (width >= 720px) { };
  • @container up to here adds @container (width <= 720px) { }, which follows the width of the container (it needs an ancestor with container-type).

Quick actions:

  • layout: Flex row, Flex column, Grid 2, Grid 3, Grid 4, Auto grid, Center, Space between, Centered stack;
  • spacing: gap, padding or margin with the framework's scale;
  • px → rem: converts pixel values to rem (16 px base).

Declarations that are already there are updated in place.

Named media queries #

Instead of repeating (width < 64em) in a hundred rules, give the condition a name and use the name. You find them in the Styles and selectors → Named media queries section, and you add them with + Media query.

NameDefault condition
mobile(width < 40em)
tablet(width < 64em)
desktop(width >= 64em)

You use them like this, in container queries too:

@media (--tablet) { padding: var(--space-s); }
@container card (--tablet) { flex-direction: column; }

Change the condition in one place and it changes everywhere. Named media queries also appear as buttons above the canvas and in the Mini GUI's Width menu. They are versioned like everything else: they go live with the release. An unknown name stays as written and the browser ignores that rule.

Global sheets #

The Global sheet pill opens the CSS that applies to the whole site. Write only what is truly global there: resets, variables in :root, tag styling. A block's styling is better off in its selector. With Full CSS you see, read-only, the sheet and the CSS of each selector as they reach the page.

From the sheet to the selector #

In imported sites, for example from Etch, much of the CSS may sit in a global sheet. When you select an element whose class has rules in the sheet, the panel shows two views:

  • In the sheet (N): only the rules that name that class, one per editor, with their context (@media …). Every change goes back into the sheet exactly where that rule was;
  • Own CSS: the selector's CSS.

At the top of the In the sheet view, As SCSS in the selector shows those rules already gathered in nested form. Move into the selector takes them out of the sheet and puts them into the class's CSS, in a single step you can undo with Ctrl+Z. From then on the class is read and edited in one place, with the Mini GUI, states and widths.

To do the same with all the blocks at once, use Into selectors… (on the sheet's pill or in the Styles and selectors section): a preview tells you how many blocks and rules move and which ones stay, then Move does everything in one step.

A rule stays in the sheet when moving it would change which rule wins, or when it is shared with other classes (.a, .b). That way the page doesn't change appearance. Before publishing, the visual comparison in the Publish… window confirms it.

The selector manager #

The Styles and selectors section lists all the site's selectors, including classes defined only in the global sheets.

  • Search for a selector, sort by Name, Usage or Size, filter by origin (All, Entries, Global sheets) or show Unused only.
  • For each selector you see where it is used and its CSS, editable as in the CSS panel.
  • Rename everywhere changes the name of the class and its BEM parts (card to box also changes card__title and card--dark, but not card-alt) in the styles, in the global sheets and in all pages. You see a preview first; the rename is refused, with the reason, if the new name is already in use or if the class appears where you can't edit it, for example in the theme files.
  • If the class also appears in free HTML (hand-written HTML blocks, Gutenberg blocks), the preview shows each place with before and after: tick the ones to rename along with the rest. You need permission to write unfiltered HTML.
  • Delete selector and Delete unused remove only the entries that no document, template or safelist uses.
  • A class that only a script, a prop or a widget adds looks unused: Used by scripts, props or widgets: always keep it puts it in the safelist (you need permission to change the settings), and Remove from the safelist takes it out.

Renaming and deleting work on the saved styles: with unsaved changes, Save now (in the note) or Save now and rename (after the preview) save first. They become changes in development, so they go live with the release and can be undone. The manager doesn't see classes added by scripts, props or widgets: check before deleting.

The single file #
