The Single file section shows the page, or the component, as a single file, modeled on Vue and Svelte files:

  • <template>: the HTML, the same as in the HTML panel;
  • <style>: one rule for each selector used in the document that has its own CSS, with media queries and states inside;
  • <script>: the document's JavaScript (editable only with permission to write unfiltered HTML);
  • for components, the props in JSON.

Apply brings everything into the document together, after checking the whole file: if there is an error, it tells you the line and changes nothing. Reload from the document takes the document back as it is. Changes go to development with the next save.

Styles apply across the whole site: removing a rule from the file doesn't delete the style, it just stops showing here.

The CSS that reaches your pages #

Histeon sends a page only the CSS it can use: a rule is included if the page has an element with the classes and ids it names. This applies to your selectors, to the global sheets and, by default, to the framework's sheet too. Rules on tags, variables in :root, fonts and animations are always included. The CSS is written directly into the page, so there are no extra files to download.

In the editor and on the canvas, on the other hand, everything is always included.

The options are in Histeon → Settings → CSS loading:

  • Framework on demand (default): each page gets only the framework rules it uses;
  • Full framework: the whole sheet on every page, as the framework would load it;
  • Global sheets in a cached file: the global sheets come whole in a file the browser keeps from page to page, while the selectors' CSS stays in the page. Worth it with large global sheets, such as imported ones. A sheet with relative url() addresses, and the static copies of the site, stay as before;
  • Classes and IDs always included (the safelist): for classes and ids that a script adds after loading and that Histeon therefore doesn't see in the page, such as .is-open or #modal. You can use the asterisk: .is-*.

If a menu or a window opened by JavaScript loses its styling on the site but not in the builder, a class is almost always missing from the safelist.

WordPress blocks with the site's style #

Inside pages you can also use WordPress's ordinary blocks: paragraphs, buttons, groups, images, quotes. With Histeon CSS their controls offer the site's colours, text sizes, spacing and shadows, and Styles shows ready-made variants: solid or outline button, group as a section or a card, rounded image, quote. They follow the site's design: if you change the colours or sizes in the Histeon CSS panel, they change too.

Histeon CSS

Histeon CSS is Histeon's free CSS framework and design system. From a few values (the brand colors, the minimum and maximum sizes of text and spacing) it calculates a complete palette, fluid scales that adapt to the screen, radii, shadows and fonts. On top of that it adds utility classes and ready-made recipes. It is included in Histeon, turned on by default and requires no license.

What it is #

Histeon CSS is made of three parts:

  • tokens, CSS variables with the --xb- prefix: var(--xb-primary), var(--xb-space-m), var(--xb-text-l);
  • utility classes, with the xb- prefix and always with a single hyphen: xb-grid-3, xb-text-l, xb-btn xb-btn-primary;
  • recipes, groups of declarations to expand in the CSS panel: @centra, @card, @tronca.

Histeon CSS doesn't style bare elements (h1, a, button): turning it on for an existing site changes nothing until you use its classes or tokens. And thanks to on-demand CSS, each page gets only the classes that page uses.

The Histeon CSS panel #

The panel opens over any page, on the site or in the admin, without reloading it:

  • from the Histeon CSS item in the admin bar;
  • with Alt+Shift+H;
  • by adding #xb-histos-css to the page address.

It has six tabs:

TabWhat you do there
ColorsThe eight color families; a click on the strip opens the 11 shades and the 9 transparencies, and a click on a swatch copies its variable
TextThe text scale, with a preview of each step
SpaceThe spacing scale and the reference widths, with a bar for each step
ShapesRadius and widths, with a preview of radii and shadows
FontsThe three font families, with a preview
RecipesSearch for a recipe, read it, copy it

Every change shows immediately on the page in front of you, but it isn't saved until you press Save to development: the new values become a change in development and go live with the next release, like everything else.

  • A changed option has a dot next to its name, and ↺ takes it back to its default. The dot shows on the tab too.
  • Search an option finds an option by name, in Italian or in English: «line length» finds «Text measure».
  • Export downloads the settings as a JSON file, with only the changed values; Import reads them back from a file, once the server has checked them. Then you save them as usual.
  • Export tokens (DTCG) downloads the computed tokens in the Design Tokens Community Group format (version 2025.10), to open in Figma or Penpot: tokens.chiaro.json (light) and, with the dark theme on, tokens.scuro.json (dark). Colours, sizes in px or rem, fonts, weights and shadows; fluid sizes (clamp()), units the format does not admit (em, ch…) and other values stay out and are listed, with their CSS, in the file itself.
  • Import tokens (DTCG) reads the tokens exported by a design tool: from Figma one file per variable mode («Export modes»), from Penpot the file of Tokens → Export. You can choose several files at once; a file with dark or scuro in its name is the dark theme, and a Penpot file with themes already carries its own. The family colours (primary, secondary, neutral, the states), the fonts, the base text size, the weight and line height of headings and the radius become parameters of the panel, over the values already there; the server checks them as for Import. Shades are not copied: Histeon CSS computes them from the family colour. Text sizes never become fixed lengths: the Report of the imported tokens, under the message, gives for each the nearest step (--xb-text-l, --xb-h2) and what was left out, with the reason. In the builder the other tokens (other colours, spaces, shadows) join the Site variables. Then you save as usual.
  • Defaults takes everything back to the Histeon CSS values, after a confirmation.

It is the same panel as the builder's Design system section (in WordPress and in Histeon Publish) and as the Histeon CSS plugin's.

The panel is visible only to users who can build with Histeon. If the site uses another framework, a notice says so and takes you to the settings.

Motion and effects #

In the same tab, Motion and effects sets the motion classes, CSS only:

  • on entering and leaving the view while scrolling: xb-enter-fade, xb-enter-up, -down, -left, -right, -grow, -shrink, -blur (and xb-exit-…). Choose where in the view the entrance is complete, the distance and the easing. In browsers that cannot tie an animation to the scroll the elements simply stay still;
  • on hover: xb-hover-lift, -shadow, -glow, -scale, -bright, -fade and xb-hover-underline (also -center and -right), with duration, lift and scale.

You can also set the starting size of grow and shrink, the blur of blur, where the exit is complete, the brightness of bright and the opacity of fade.

For those who ask their system for less motion, everything stays still.

Advanced #

At the end of the Forms and details tab, Advanced chooses which groups of variables to print: transparencies, shadows, buttons and links, forms, cards, icons, motion. A group switched off is not in the site's sheet, which gets lighter; the classes that use it go back to their own values or do nothing, and the CSS panel flags its variables as unknown. In the same way you choose the groups of classes to print (colours, buttons, motion, icons, cards, forms): a group switched off is not in the sheet and not offered in the CSS panel. Another switch leaves the theme's palette to the block editor instead of Histeon CSS's.

There too, Tokens inline prints the tokens in the page instead of loading a file (WordPress only: in Histeon Publish all the CSS is one sheet already). A group of variables or classes switched off shows at once in the preview.

Forms and details #

In the panel's Forms and details tab:

  • Forms: padding, radius, background, border, focus ring, text size and placeholder of the fields; label size and weight; help size and colour; error and valid colours; checkboxes and radios (also as a switch); gap between fields. With the default styles also the dropdown arrow and field groups (fieldset). xb-input fields always follow them; with Default form styles also fields without a class and Fluent Forms forms, whose submit button becomes like the site's buttons;
  • Accessibility and details: a focus ring across the site, smooth scrolling (never for those who ask for less motion), reduced motion when the system asks for it (these three are on to start with), selection colour, quote style and text columns (class xb-columns).

Step bar: with the default form styles, the bar of Fluent Forms' multi-page forms takes the height, colour and track chosen here.

Shapes, buttons and links #

Besides what follows: every shadow is set by step (offset, blur, opacity); buttons switch off by family or by variant (outline, light, dark), and the classes switched off leave the sheet; the light and dark variants choose their hover shade; a surface can have a name of its own (xb-surface-<name>) and be a gradient, with text that reads at both ends; there are two more overlays (xb-overlay-2, -3) and the card title (xb-card-title) with its own size; icons have their own sizes; icon lists choose their symbol; external links show ↗ (or only a text for screen readers), once you write the site's domain.

In the panel's Shapes tab:

  • Default border and dividers: width and style of the xb-border border; width, style and space of the xb-divider dividers; the ratio of the radius scale;
  • Buttons: padding, radius (up to a pill), weight, line height and text size, case, letter spacing, border, minimum width, and full width on phones. They apply to every button: xb-btn, each family's variants (xb-btn-primary, xb-btn-primary-outline, xb-btn-primary-light, xb-btn-primary-dark, and the same for secondary, tertiary, accent, neutral and the states) and the sizes from xb-btn-xs to xb-btn-xl. On hover a filled button takes the shade chosen in the palette;
  • Links: underline (always, on hover, never), thickness and offset of the line; with Default link styles they also apply to links without a class.

Also in the Shapes tab:

  • Cards: padding, inner gap, radius, background, shadow, border and media aspect ratio. Inside an xb-card, xb-card-media puts an image edge to edge with that ratio and xb-card-body lays out the text with the chosen gap; a button at the end stays at the bottom. xb-radius-inner gives a nested element the right (concentric) radius;
  • Icons: colour and stroke width, sizes from xb-icon-xs to xb-icon-2xl, a boxed icon (xb-icon-boxed) and lists with a check mark (xb-list-icon).

Also in the Shapes tab, Overlays and surfaces: colour, opacity and blend of the xb-overlay overlay (and of its graded xb-overlay-gradient), and three site surfaces (xb-surface-1, -2, -3) with their background, border and shadow: the text on them turns white or black by itself, whichever reads.

There are also text shadows (xb-text-shadow-s, -m, -l) and drop-shadow shadows for images and transparent SVGs (xb-drop-shadow-s, -m, -l).

Space and layout #

Text spacing can apply to all the content (main), not only to xb-prose, and sections can sit on the content grid by default: both start off, and any class of the site wins over them.

In the panel's Space tab, besides the scale:

  • Sections: the multiplier of the sections' vertical space (small and large screen), the default padding of xb-section and the side gutter in pixels (at 0 the space m applies);
  • Gaps between elements: between a section's children, between the blocks of xb-stack, in the grids; for xb-grid-auto the minimum column and whether the columns fill the row or keep their place;
  • Texts: in an xb-prose text, the gap between blocks, before and after headings and between list items;
  • Layout: the boxed layout (the page narrow and centred on a background, with a shadow), minimum site width, header height (--xb-header-height, used by --xb-sticky-top for sticky elements) and Anchors below the header (on to start with), so a link to a section does not hide it behind a fixed header.

The content grid works with the xb-content-grid class on the container: its children sit in the content column, xb-wide takes them to the wide width, xb-full edge to edge, xb-narrow to the text measure.

Typography #

Headings can be italic; each level also has its own letter spacing and max width. Each text step (xb-text-xs … xb-text-4xl) can have its own line height, letter spacing, max width, case and wrapping. In Site fonts, the Accent and Display buttons give a site font to the two free roles.

In the panel's Text tab, besides the text scale:

  • Separate heading scale (on to start with): headings H1-H6 get their own base and ratio; off, they follow the text scale;
  • Headings: font (one of the five roles: body, headings, code, accent, display), weight, line height, letter spacing, case, wrapping (balanced to start with) and max width. They apply to the xb-h1 … xb-h6 classes; with Default heading styles also to the site's h1 … h6 headings;
  • Headings by level: weight, line height, colour, font and case of one level only, for example all H2 lighter and in the primary colour («As all» or 0 = the value of all headings);
  • Sizes of single steps: a text step or a heading with its own two sizes in pixels (small and large screen), outside the scale;
  • Text and root: line height and wrapping of the text (with Default text styles they apply to the page body and paragraphs) and the root size, which changes every rem measure: use it only if you know why.

The Fonts tab has two free roles, Accent and Display, for the headings or with var(--xb-font-accent) and var(--xb-font-display).

Colors #

There are eight families: Primary, Secondary, Accent, Neutral, Success, Warning, Danger and Info. For each one, choose the color with the picker or type a value (hexadecimal, rgb() or oklch()).

From each color Histeon CSS calculates:

  • the base color: --xb-primary;
  • eleven shades, from --xb-primary-50 (the lightest) to --xb-primary-950 (the darkest);
  • the color of readable text on top of it: --xb-on-primary.

Shades are calculated in the OKLCH color space, which keeps the hue and makes lightness uniform across different families.

In the panel's Colors tab you also find:

  • Tertiary, a ninth family, off until you switch it on; the switch next to the name also turns off secondary, accent and the state families you do not use (their variables go away);
  • Palette: one lightness for the brand families or for the state ones, so they all weigh the same; the hover shade (--xb-primary-hover and the like, 600 to start with);
  • Colour roles: for background, surfaces, text, headings, border, links and focus pick a family or a shade, or leave «Automatic». With the automatic contrast relationships, an automatic text that does not read on its background moves by itself to the nearest shade that does;
  • Dark theme: when on, the site has the dark roles and colours. The site scheme says whether it stays light (dark only where you put xb-scheme-dark), is always dark or follows the visitor's system. Each family can have its own dark colour, or keep the automatic one, and you choose the dark theme's hover shade.

The Readability list checks the dark theme's pairs too.

Then there are the role colors, which point to the right shades: --xb-bg, --xb-surface, --xb-text, --xb-text-muted, --xb-heading, --xb-border, --xb-link, --xb-focus and others. With the xb-scheme-dark class on a container, these colors switch to the dark shades.

Fluid text and spacing #

Text and spacing are fluid: they grow with the screen width between a minimum and a maximum, without media queries. You choose a few values, Histeon CSS calculates all the steps.

ParameterDefault
Text: size of step mfrom 16 to 18 px
Text: ratio between one step and the nextfrom 1.2 to 1.25
Spacing: size of step mfrom 16 to 24 px
Spacing: ratio between one step and the nextfrom 1.5 to 1.6
Reference screen widthsfrom 360 to 1440 px

The first value applies on narrow screens, the second on wide ones; in between, the value grows continuously.

The steps you get:

  • text: from --xb-text-xs to --xb-text-4xl, plus --xb-h1 … --xb-h6 for headings;
  • spacing: from --xb-space-3xs to --xb-space-4xl, plus the section spacing (--xb-section-s, -m, -l, -xl) and the side margin --xb-gutter.

Shapes, widths and fonts #

  • Radii: --xb-radius-s, -m, -l, -xl and -full, calculated from the radius of step m (8 px by default).
  • Shadows: --xb-shadow-s, -m, -l, -xl.
  • Widths: --xb-width-content (72 rem), --xb-width-wide (90 rem) and --xb-width-prose (65 characters, the comfortable measure for reading).
  • Fonts: --xb-font-body, --xb-font-heading and --xb-font-mono. Write the names separated by commas, with quotes around those that contain spaces.

To serve fonts from your own site, without requesting them from Google on every visit, use Histeon → Settings → Local fonts: import a family from Google Fonts or upload a WOFF2 file. Then write its name in the panel.

The generator and the Design system in the builder #

In the builder, the Design system section shows all the variables of the active framework with their real value: a swatch for colors, a bar for spacing, “Aa” at its size for text, a square for radii, a card for shadows. A click copies var(--name); the search filters by name.

With Histeon CSS active, the same section contains the Histeon CSS generator: colors, scales and shapes from a few values, with the changes visible right away on the canvas. Below the colors, Readability checks the contrast of the text and background pairs, which must be at least 4.5:1, and says “all readable” or how many pairs need a second look. Save in development keeps the changes; Back to defaults starts over; ↺ next to a token resets it to its default value.

Colors are shown by family: only the main color of each one appears (primary, secondary, accent, neutral, the states and the roles such as text and background). The button with the dots next to it opens that family's shades, transparencies and text color. Searching for a name shows every color that contains it.

Site variables. ＋ Variable adds a variable of your own, next to Histeon CSS's. Its name becomes --xb-site-<name>: lower-case letters, digits and hyphens, up to 40 characters. The value follows the token rules: numbers, units, colors, calc(), clamp(), var(). The canvas shows it at once; Save in development keeps it, and the release puts it online. It appears in the CSS panel's completions. ✕ deletes it. Import variables adds many from one file: variabili-sito.json written by Histeon Publish, or the tokens exported by Figma or Penpot (those without a design parameter). A variable of the same name takes the new value; the maximum is 200.

Recipes. The same section lists every recipe with where it comes from: Histeon, Automatic.css (imported) or Site. A click opens a recipe's CSS, which you can change: the site's version replaces Histeon's recipe with the same name, and ↺ Back to the original recipe brings it back. ＋ Recipe adds a new one, usable at once with @name in the CSS panel and in the Recipes panel. A recipe cannot contain markup, url(), @import or anything else that loads or runs something, and is at most 4 KB.

Utility classes #

FamilyExamples
Containersxb-container, xb-container-wide, xb-container-prose, xb-section, xb-section-l
Layoutxb-stack, xb-cluster, xb-flow, xb-center, xb-grid-2 … xb-grid-6, xb-grid-auto
Spacingxb-p-m, xb-px-l, xb-py-s, xb-mt-xl, xb-mb-0, xb-gap-m
Textxb-text-l, xb-h2, xb-font-heading, xb-weight-700, xb-align-center, xb-balance, xb-prose
Colorsxb-color-primary, xb-bg-primary-700 (with readable text), xb-border-neutral, xb-scheme-dark
Shapesxb-rounded-m, xb-shadow-l, xb-border
Componentsxb-btn, xb-btn-primary, xb-btn-outline, xb-btn-l, xb-card, xb-badge, xb-link
Accessibility and utilitiesxb-visually-hidden, xb-skip-link, xb-clickable-parent, xb-cover, xb-aspect-video, xb-overlay

The xb-grid-2 … xb-grid-6 grids reduce the number of columns by themselves when there isn't enough space. In the HTML panel, inside class="…", the builder suggests these classes as you type.

Recipes #

In the CSS panel, type @ plus the name at the start of a declaration, and the recipe expands into a group of declarations.

GroupRecipes
Layout@centra, @flex-centro, @pila, @fila, @fila-spaziata, @griglia-auto, @griglia-2, @griglia-3, @griglia-4, @contenitore, @sezione, @tutta-larghezza, @flusso, @appiccicoso, @scorri-orizzontale
Position and media@riempi, @immagine-copri, @aspetto-video, @aspetto-quadrato, @sovrapposizione, @link-esteso
Text@tronca, @tronca-righe, @bilancia, @misura, @testo-gradiente, @titolo
Components@card, @solleva, @vetro, @divisore, @pulsante-reset, @lista-pulita
Accessibility and motion@nascosto-accessibile, @focus, @transizione, @meno-movimento, @barra-nascosta

Recipes use the --xb-* tokens with a fallback value, so they work with any framework.

With other frameworks #

Histeon also works with Core Framework and Automatic.css, if you have installed them yourself: Histeon doesn't include them. You choose the framework in Histeon → Settings → CSS framework. With Core Framework or Automatic.css, a button in the builder's left column opens their panel over the canvas.

The --xb-* tokens act as a bridge: Histeon links them to the variables of the active framework (for example --xb-space-m becomes var(--space-m)). A component written with the tokens therefore works with any framework, without changes.

Switching frameworks #

Histeon → Settings → Framework switch with migration rewrites the native names of the current framework (variables and classes) into those of the new one, in pages, components and styles.

  1. In Switch to, choose the new framework.
  2. Leave Carry over the variables and rules of … that the site uses turned on: the old framework's variables and rules stay available, so nothing breaks.
  3. Migration preview shows, document by document, what would change and which names have no match. It doesn't change anything.
  4. Prepare in development prepares everything as a change in development.
  5. Check the preview and publish from the builder. A single release takes content and framework live together, and restoring the previous release also brings back the framework.

From Automatic.css to Histeon CSS #

If the site uses Automatic.css, you can switch to Histeon CSS without changing how the pages look. The migration reads the values from the sheets Automatic.css generated on the site.

Follow the steps in “Switching frameworks”, choosing Histeon CSS in Switch to. Here is what happens:

  1. The values. All the Automatic.css variables are kept in a global sheet, and the Histeon CSS tokens point to them: the pages use the same values as before. Before publishing, check them with the visual comparison.
  2. The palette. The Automatic.css base colors (primary, secondary, accent, neutral) become the colors of the Histeon CSS palette.
  3. The names. Classes and variables with an exact equivalent are renamed: text--l becomes xb-text-l, var(--space-m) becomes var(--xb-space-m), hidden-accessible becomes xb-visually-hidden.
  4. The rest. Classes without an identical equivalent (btn--*, bg--*, section--*…) keep their name and their rule, served from a compatibility sheet.
  5. The recipes the site had in Automatic.css come along too, if Automatic.css is still active during the import, and stay alongside those of Histeon CSS (when names are the same, the site's one wins).

After the release, the pages take their values and classes from Histeon CSS and the compatibility sheet.

Automatic.cssHisteon CSS
ultra-light50
light200
semi-light300
base colorbase (500)
hover600
semi-dark700
dark800
ultra-dark950

The Automatic.css base family becomes neutral in Histeon CSS, if the site doesn't already have one.

Importing is recommended, not required. Without importing, the sheets already on the site stay loaded until you import, so the site stays as it was. An admin notice reminds you about the import: Import now takes you to the settings, Later hides it for 14 days.

If you import an Etch site that uses Automatic.css, the import page asks you where its design system should go: Into Histeon CSS (recommended), Into Core Framework (if it is installed) or Stay on Automatic.css.

From Automatic.css to Core Framework #

If you prefer Core Framework, the From Automatic.css to Core Framework, everything included section uses Create the Core Framework project to create a new project with the colors and all the variables of Automatic.css, plus a compatibility sheet with the rules the site uses. Then open Core Framework and press Save changes, switch to Core Framework with Prepare in development and publish.

Histeon CSS as a standalone plugin #

Histeon CSS also exists as a plugin of its own (histos-css), for sites that don't use the builder: for example a site made with the block editor and any theme.

It offers the tokens, the classes, the recipes and the same popup panel (admin bar or Alt+Shift+H), with live preview and the readability check. In the block editor, the palette and the text sizes become those of the site. Appearance → Histeon CSS explains where to find everything.

The differences compared with Histeon CSS inside Histeon:

  • there is no development and no release: saving in the panel applies the values to the site immediately;
  • the panel is reserved for those who can change the site's appearance (usually administrators);
  • uninstalling the plugin deletes its values.

If you activate Histeon on a site that already has the Histeon CSS plugin, the plugin stays idle and a notice tells you that you can deactivate it: Histeon CSS is already included.

Components

A component is a piece of a page you build once and use wherever you like: an event card, a header, a button with an icon, a contact block. It has its own props (the values that change from one use to another) and its own slots (the places where other content goes). When you edit it, it changes everywhere it is used.

How it works #

Every component has:

  • a name, the one you read in the menus, for example “Event card”;
  • a stable key, lowercase with hyphens, for example event-card or ui/card-hero. The key doesn't change when you move the site, export it or import it elsewhere;
  • a definition: the markup, the styles of its classes, possibly some JavaScript, the props and the slots.

In the HTML panel a component is written with the key's name in capitals: event-card becomes <EventCard>, ui/card-hero becomes <Ui.CardHero>. The capital initial is what distinguishes a component from an HTML tag.

The site's components are in the builder's Components section and in Histeon → Components.

Creating a component #

From scratch #

  1. In the top bar press New component (or New component… in the command palette).
  2. Enter the Key (for example ui/card-hero) and the Name.
  3. Press Create and open.

The empty component opens for editing right away, over the document you were working on.

From an element on the page #

A component often starts from something you have already built:

  1. Select the element, for example the card you want to reuse.
  2. In the Properties press Create component…, or choose the same item in the Structure's context menu.
  3. Enter the Name: the Key is suggested automatically (“Event card” becomes event-card), and you can change it.
  4. Press Create.

The element and all its content become the component's definition, and an instance of the component stays in its place on the page.

Create component… is disabled when the selected part uses values that exist only there, such as the variable of an outer loop (post). A component sees only the general data (this, site, user, url, data) and its props: values that come from outside must be passed as props. Variables of loops that are inside the selection are fine.

Props #

Props are the values that change from one use to another: the card's title, the image, the link. While you edit a component, the Component props icon in the left column opens their panel:

  • Component key, with an example of how it is written in code;
  • the list of props, each with its type, an optional default value and an optional sample; ✕ removes it;
  • a new prop field with the Add button.
TypeWhat for
stringA short text
numberA number
booleanTrue or false, for example a highlighted variant
urlAn address
imageAn image
rich-textA formatted text
enumA choice among fixed values
arrayA list, to repeat with a loop
objectA group of values

In the component's code, props are read with {props.name}:

<article class="card">
  <img src={props.image.url} alt={props.image.alt}>
  <h3 class="card__title">{props.title}</h3>
  {#if props.featured}<span class="card__badge">Featured</span>{/if}
  {@slot}
</article>

Sample content #

A component open by itself has no page to give it its props, and it is often made to be filled: without help the canvas would be blank. So, only in the builder's canvas:

  • each prop takes its sample, if any, else its default, else a value from its type: the prop's name in words for a text (“mainTitle” becomes “Main title”), a placeholder image, the first choice of an enum, “true” for a boolean (so what the prop shows is visible);
  • elements with the hidden attribute are shown, with a dashed outline and the label “hidden on the page”;
  • an empty box shows a placeholder: “empty”, or “empty: a script fills it” when the component has JavaScript (for example a table of contents a script fills in).

None of this ends up in the saved component or in pages: pages use the default, or the value they give.

Variants: switches for whoever uses the component #

A “dark card” or a “large card” does not need a second component: it is a variant. In the component panel, under Variants, type the name (for example Dark) and press Add a variant. Histeon:

  • adds a yes/no prop dark, off by default;
  • gives the first element the class card--dark when the prop is on (class="card {props.dark ? "card--dark" : ""}");
  • prepares the CSS of .card--dark with the variables .card declares, to redefine: for example --background: black; instead of --background: white;.

The variant's CSS is in the CSS panel, among the element's pills. In the pages the variant is a switch among the props, in the builder and in the block editor; in the builder it can be turned on right away, before the component is published. The component's first element needs a class: the variant's class is made from it.

Slots #

A slot is the place where whoever uses the component can put whatever content they want: a paragraph, a button, another component.

  • In the definition, {@slot} marks the main slot. You also find it in the Add menu as Slot (in components).
  • You can have several named slots: {@slot media}, {@slot actions}.
  • A slot that nobody fills shows nothing. To hide an empty container, check {#if !slots.actions.empty}…{/if}.

Using a component #

To insert a component into a page:

  • from the Add menu, in the Components group (you can search for it by name or by key);
  • or by writing it in the HTML panel.

In code, props are passed as attributes, and the content between the opening and closing tags goes into the main slot:

<Card title="Summer concert" featured>
  <p>Saturday at 9 pm, free entry.</p>
</Card>

To fill a named slot, use {#slot name}:

<Card title={post.title}>
  {#slot media}<img src={post.image.url} alt={post.image.alt}>{/slot}
  <p>{post.excerpt}</p>
</Card>

A value in quotes is a text (title="Hello"), even with expressions inside (title="Hello {user.name}"); a value in curly braces is an expression (title={post.title}, count={3}); a prop written without a value counts as “true” (featured). Inside a loop, pass the loop's values to the component: <Card title={post.title}>.

If you select an instance, the Properties panel shows its Props: for each one, choose whether it is a Text or an Expression and enter the value.

Editing a component #

A component is edited in place, without leaving the page:

  • double-click the component on the canvas or in the Structure;
  • Edit the component in the Structure's context menu;
  • the Edit the component button in the instance's Properties.

While you edit, all the panels (Structure, HTML, CSS, JavaScript, Props) work on the component's definition. At the top the path appears, for example Home › Component Card, along with two buttons:

  • Save and exit saves the component to development, styles included, and goes back to the page: the canvas shows the new version right away and the instance you started from is selected again;
  • Exit without saving discards the changes (after a confirmation, if there are any) and goes back.

Ctrl+S saves without exiting. A component inside another component opens over the first one, and the path at the top lets you go back up one or more levels.

While the component is open, the page stays in memory exactly as it was: unsaved changes, selection and undo history. When you exit you find everything again, including Ctrl+Z on the changes made before.

Changes to a component apply everywhere the component is used. Before changing its structure, check which pages it appears on.

A component in use is not deleted #

If a component is used by a page, a template or another component, live or only in development, it cannot be moved to the trash or deleted: not from wp-admin, not from the block editor. The message says where it is used, for example The component “Card” is in use and cannot be deleted. Remove it first from: Home, About us (in development). Remove it from those pages, save, then delete it.

Styles and JavaScript #

A component's styles are those of its classes, written in the CSS panel as for any other element (see the Styles chapter). They apply across the whole site and reach a page only if the page uses the component. For styles that work with any framework, use the Histeon CSS --xb-* tokens.

In the JavaScript panel you can write a JavaScript module for the component: it goes into the page as <script type="module">, after the content, and doesn't run on the canvas. JavaScript can be written only with WordPress's unfiltered HTML permission; for other users the panel is read-only.

How it is published #

A component follows the same rules as pages:

  1. Save and exit (or Ctrl+S) saves the component to development. The public site doesn't change.
  2. The page's Preview already shows the new version of the component, so you can check it where it is used.
  3. Publish… lists the component among the changes to take live, together with pages and styles. With the release, the component changes on all the pages that use it at the same moment.

In the Publish… window, a component has no page of its own to compare: the visual comparison shows it through the page open in the builder. If something goes wrong, restoring the previous release also brings back the component.

A component created from an element exists right away, but no live page uses it until you save and publish the page you created it in.

The section library #

A successful section, such as a hero, a card grid or a footer, can be saved to reuse in other pages or on another site.

  1. Select the element on the canvas or in the structure.
  2. Open the Library section in the left bar, type a name and press Save to the library. The styles of its classes are saved with the element.
  3. To reuse it press Insert: the paste dialog opens, the same as for copy and paste between sites, showing new, identical or conflicting classes.
  4. With Export you download the whole library as a .xblibrary file; on another Histeon site you load it with Import…. Sections already there are not duplicated.

Images stay at the source site's addresses. To really move them use the site move, or upload them to the new site's media library.

Having the agent rebuild it #

Have the agent rebuild it, next to a library section, sends the connected AI agent a request: rebuild the section in this page with the project's classes and tokens. The agent does not get the markup to copy but its description: the parts, their role, and the layout with the project token nearest to each size and colour. The request shows in the ✦ panel.

Content and data

A Histeon page doesn't contain only hand-written text: it can show the title of the content being viewed, the latest posts, this week's events, the price of a product. This chapter explains where data comes from and how to put it on the page.

Expressions: placeholders in curly braces #

A piece of data is written in curly braces, inside a text or an attribute: {this.title} becomes the title of the current content, {site.name} the name of the site. In the HTML panel you write them by hand; in the data panels there is always a ready-made expression to copy or to insert into the selected element.

The roots available on every page:

RootWhat it containsExamples
thisThe content being viewed (post, page, product…){this.title}, {this.url}, {this.excerpt}, {this.date}, {this.image.url}, {this.author.name}, {this.categories}
siteThe site{site.name}, {site.description}, {site.url}, {site.year}, {site.logo}
userThe visitor{user.loggedIn}, {user.name}
urlThe page address{url.path}, {url.query.q}
dataQueries and JSON sources read without a loop{data.hours[0].day}
facetsThe loop filters{facets.events.total}

Inside a loop you also get the variable you chose (usually item) and index, the position in the loop; inside a component there are also props and slots.

Only url.path travels with Histeon Publish (from Histeon 0.5.4): in Publish it is the path of the page being built, on the site the path of the request, the same on a static site. url.query, user and facets stay on the site: content that uses them is not sent to Histeon Publish (chapter 12).

Some useful rules:

  • No errors on screen. A name that doesn't exist, an empty field or an error count as "nothing": the page stays up and that spot stays empty.
  • Fallback values. {this.excerpt ?? "No excerpt"} uses the text in quotes when the value is missing; .default("…") also kicks in on an empty text or on zero.
  • Comparisons. ==, !=, <, >, &&, ||, ! and the conditional cond ? "yes" : "no". There are no calculations or free function calls: it is a language for showing data, not for programming.
  • Security. Every value comes out sanitized: a piece of data never becomes markup, so it can't break the page or inject code.

Modifiers #

A modifier transforms the value and is written after a dot: {this.title.upper()}.

ModifierWhat it does
inline()Prints the text with inline formats only (bold, italics, links): for rich text props
upper(), lower(), capitalize(), trim(), stripTags()Uppercase, lowercase, capital initial, spaces, tags
truncateWords(20), truncateChars(120)Shorten the text and add "…"
date("j F Y")Formats a date; without a format it uses the site's
number(2)Number with the separators of the site's language
length(), first(), last(), join(", "), slice(0, 3), includes(x), pluck("name")Lists and texts
groupBy("date", "Y"), groupBy("title", "letter")Splits a list into groups (key and items) by year, by initial or by a field's value, for a heading per group
default(x)x if the value is empty, false or zero
json(), slug()JSON text, URL-friendly text

A list split into groups, with a heading per group:

{#loop data.ultimiArticoli.groupBy("date", "Y") as year}
  <h2>{year.key}</h2>
  {#loop year.items as post}<p><a href={post.url}>{post.title}</a></p>{/loop}
{/loop}

The same on a query's result, with a single query: .groupBy(…) right after the call, with written texts (the field and, if needed, the mode).

{#loop notizie(limit: 50).groupBy("date", "Y") as year}
  <h2>{year.key}</h2>
  {#loop year.items as post}<p><a href={post.url}>{post.title}</a></p>{/loop}
{/loop}

After the query only one groupBy is allowed: no other modifiers. In Gutenberg the same is chosen in the loop's panel, under In groups; in the builder, in Data and queries, under In groups in Use it on the page (see “Using a query on the page”).

Without writing them: Insert a piece of data #

In the Properties panel, under Content, the { } Insert a piece of data button composes the expression for you:

  1. Data: what to show. The list follows the place in the page: inside a loop the fields of its item come first (title, date, author, image…), in a component its props, then the page, the site and the visitor.
  2. Format: how to show it. A date as “4 November 2026” or “04/11/2026”, a number with decimals, the first words of a text, a list with its separator, an image's address or alternative text.
  3. If empty, show: the fallback text.
  4. Text before and Text after: the words around it, for example “Published on ”.

Below you see how it is written in code, for example Published on {this.date.date("j F Y")}; Add to the content puts it at the end of the text. For a component prop in Expression mode, Compose the expression does the same and reads back the expression already there, when the composer wrote it.

Conditions #

A Condition shows part of the page only if an expression is true. You add it from the Add menu (Ctrl+I) → Condition, or in the HTML panel:

{#if this.image}
  <img src={this.image.url} alt={this.image.alt}>
{:else if user.loggedIn}
  <p>Add a featured image.</p>
{:else}
  <p>No image.</p>
{/if}

In the Structure panel the condition appears as Condition, with its branches: if … for each test and else for the last one. false, nothing, 0, an empty text and an empty list are false; everything else is true.

There is no "empty" branch for a loop with no results: wrap the loop in a condition, for example {#if data.events.length()}.

Loops and queries #

A loop repeats a piece of the page for each result: a card for each post, a row for each opening time. The results come from a query, that is, a search saved with a name in the builder's Data and queries section.

The default queries are always available and don't need to be created:

QueryWhat it returns
postsContent (posts, pages, custom types)
termsCategories and terms
usersAuthors with published content
mainThe query of the page being viewed: an archive, a search

Creating a query #

  1. Open Data and queries and press New query. Choose the type: Content, Categories and terms, Users or Of the page.
  2. In the Visual view, choose what to show: content type, Filters by category, tag or taxonomy (is one of, is not, has all), authors, text, Order (by custom field too), How many, specific content or content to exclude.
  3. On the right you see the data right away: the first 20 records as cards and, for each one, the keys with their value and the ready-made expression ({item.title}, {item.image.url}, {item.author.name}).
  4. Save: the query is saved in development and reaches the site with the next release.

The Code view shows the same arguments in JSON. Arguments the visual view doesn't know about stay intact and are edited there.

Ready queries: the gallery #

In the Queries section, From the gallery… adds a query already written with one click, to save and then edit like the others:

  • Latest posts: the most recent, sticky posts left out;
  • Related posts: other posts in the page's category, without the page itself (the loop passes this.id and the category by itself);
  • Featured products: the products marked “featured” in WooCommerce (shown only when the shop is there);
  • Upcoming events: content of the “evento” type whose “data” field is today or later, nearest first; type and field can be changed in the query.

Using a query on the page #

  • Wrap in a loop: select the element on the page to repeat (a card, a table row) and press the button: the element becomes the template, repeated for each result.
  • Insert a new loop: adds a ready-made loop, for you to edit.

In groups, above the two buttons, splits the query's result into groups: By year of publication, By month of publication or By first letter of the title (for content queries and the page's own query). It stays one query: the loop splits its result. With a grouping chosen, Wrap in a loop puts a heading for each group ({gruppo.key}, for example “2026”) and, below it, the selected element repeated for each content of the group; Insert a new loop does the same with a ready card. On the right the preview says how many groups there are and how many contents each holds. It is the same loop chosen in Gutenberg and written by hand:

{#loop latest_events(limit: 3).groupBy("date", "Y") as gruppo}
  <section>
    <h2>{gruppo.key}</h2>
    {#loop gruppo.items as item}<article><h3><a href={item.url}>{item.title}</a></h3></article>{/loop}
  </section>
{/loop}

In the HTML panel a loop is written like this:

{#loop latest_events(limit: 3) as item}
  <article>
    <h3><a href={item.url}>{item.title}</a></h3>
  </article>
{/loop}

Queries with parameters #

A query can leave the choice of some values to the loop. In the arguments, "$limit ?? 10" means "the loop's limit parameter, or else 10". That way the same latest_events query serves both the home page (latest_events(limit: 3)) and the archive (latest_events(limit: 12)). The visual view shows the values the loop can change and lets you edit their default value. -1 means the maximum allowed, that is 100.

Loops show only published content, without a password and readable by visitors, even if the query asks for something else. Content types without public pages are included in loops only if an administrator allows them in Histeon → Settings, Non-public content in loops section.

A result can also be read outside a loop, with the data root: {data.latest_events[0].title}.

JSON sources #

A JSON source contains data you write yourself: opening hours, locations, price list items. You create it in Data and queries → New JSON source and edit it as a table (rows and columns), as a list of keys or in the Code view.

  • In a loop: {#loop data.hours as h}{h.day}: {h.time}{/loop}.
  • Without a loop: {data.hours[0].day}.

Sources are versioned like the rest of the site and go live with the next release. A source and a query can't have the same name, and posts, terms, main and users are reserved.

Data from an address #

A source can also read the JSON of another service (a calendar, a price list kept elsewhere): choose From an address, write the https:// address, if needed the part to use (for example items) and every how many minutes to read it again, then Test. It is read like a source written here. For safety the site only calls the hosts an administrator allowed, next to the source; it keeps a copy for the chosen time and, when the service does not answer, shows the last good copy.

Content types, taxonomies and fields #

The builder's Types and fields section creates the structure of your content without other plugins:

  • + Content type: Events, Locations, People… with singular and plural name, technical name, address, own pages yes or no, parents (like pages), editor features (title, content, featured image, excerpt, author, comments, revisions, order and parent) and who manages it (like posts or like pages).
  • + Taxonomy: Categories (nested) or Tags, with the types it applies to.
  • + Field: the fields of a type, of posts, pages, terms or users.

The field types are Text, Long text, Number, Date, Time, Yes / no, Choice, Image, File (a PDF or another file of the library), Web address, Email, Linked content and Repeater (rows with the same fields: opening hours, stages, price list items). A row can have a repeater of its own, for example a trip's stages, each with its own times: two levels at most, and no further repeater in the second.

A choice can have several values (checkboxes) or show as radio buttons instead of the menu; an image can become a gallery, and a file or a linked content can be more than one. Several values are read with a loop: {#loop this.fields.foto as f}<img src={f.url} alt={f.alt}>{/loop}.

A new type exists in WordPress only after publishing: save, then Publish…. The builder shows the status of each type. After publishing, the technical name can no longer be changed. Only an administrator creates types and fields; other users see the fields and use their expressions.

A type without its own pages (testimonials, price list items) has no address and doesn't appear in search or in the public API: it shows only where you put it with a loop.

Filling in and reading fields #

Fields are filled in in the block editor, in the Fields panel of the sidebar. For a repeater there is Add row and the controls to move or remove rows. Term and user fields appear in the WordPress term screens and in the profile, in the “Profile fields” section.

On pages:

  • {this.fields.price} in the template of a single item;
  • {item.fields.price} in a loop;
  • {#loop this.fields.hours as r}{r.day} {r.time}{/loop} for a repeater;
  • {#loop this.fields.stages as s}{s.place}: {#loop s.times as t}{t.day}{/loop}{/loop} for a repeater inside another one's rows;
  • {this.categories[0].fields.color} for a field of a category, {this.author.fields.role} for a field of the author.

The Use them on the page block lists the expressions for the fields of the selected type, with the button to insert them.

Field groups #

When several types share the same fields (contacts, prices, opening hours), a field group writes them once: in Field groups give it a name, choose Only these types or All but, and add the fields. Every chosen type gets them and reads them as its own ({this.fields.email}). When a type already has a field with the same name, its own wins; a type created later gets the fields when you save.

Terms #

For a taxonomy, for categories and for tags, the Terms view shows the terms in a table: name, slug, parent, description and fields change in the cell, + Add creates a term (also inside another), Delete removes it after a confirmation. Changes go live at once.

The content table #

For each type (and for posts and pages) the section has two views: Definition and Content. The Content view is a table with search, a Status filter and 20 rows per page. Title and simple fields are edited directly in the cell; images, linked content and rows are edited in the WordPress editor with Edit ↗. + Add (draft) creates a new item as a draft.

Changes to content go straight to the site, as in WordPress: content has no Histeon versions. The pages and templates that show it do.

Fields from ACF, Secure Custom Fields, JetEngine and Meta Box #

If you already use one of these plugins, its field groups appear in Fields from other plugins. For each field you see whether pages can read it and a toggle to make it readable (it also works for the whole group). A field from another plugin can contain internal data, which is why it is readable only if an administrator allows it or if the plugin already exposes it in the REST API. Password fields are never readable.

The fields of options pages of Secure Custom Fields or ACF (opening hours, contacts, footer texts) appear there too, with their page. Once readable, they are used in any page as {site.options.dati_sito.orari}: the options page's name, with _ instead of dashes, then the field's. They are read only: you change them from the options page in WordPress.

You read them with this.fields.name or, to read only from one plugin, with this.acf.name, this.jetengine.name, this.metabox.name. Histeon doesn't run the plugin's formatting: rich text loses its markup and dates become YYYY-MM-DD.

Loop filters #

Loop filters let visitors choose what to see in a list: search, categories, value of a field, order and pages. They work even without JavaScript and, if it is available, they update the results without reloading the page.

  1. Open the Loop filters section and create a group with a name, for example events.
  2. Choose the content (Content of a type or one of your queries), the results Per page and the Page parameter.
  3. Add the filters: Search, Taxonomy (with multiple choices and the combination at least one or all), Field, Sorting. Each filter has a Label and a Parameter, the name that appears in the address.
  4. Try it as a visitor, then Save the filters.
  5. Insert into the page writes the complete markup: the form, the results with their count, a link to reset the filters and the pages. From there it is edited like any other element.

In expressions, filters are read as facets.events: {#loop facets.events.items as item} for the results, {facets.events.total} for the count, facets.events.pageLinks for the pages. Each option has its own count, which takes into account the other filters already chosen.

A field can be filtered only if it is readable by pages and has a simple value. On the static site the filters stay dynamic: see the chapter on the static site.

E-commerce: WooCommerce and FluentCart #

With WooCommerce or FluentCart active, a product is read with this.product.* in the product template and with item.product.* in a product loop (type product for WooCommerce, fluent-products for FluentCart).

KeyWhat it contains
priceTextThe price formatted by the store (in FluentCart also a range “€10 – €25”)
price, regularPrice, salePrice, onSalePrices as numbers, and whether it is on sale
inStock, stockStatusAvailability
sku, type, shortDescriptionCode, type, summary without markup
addToCartUrl, addToCartTextLink and text of the “add to cart” button
gallery, categoriesThe product's images and categories
rating, reviewCountRating and reviews (WooCommerce only)

FluentCart adds minPrice, maxPrice, variations (the active variations), subscription, fulfillment, brands, buyNowUrl, cartUrl and checkoutUrl.

Histeon reads only the catalog: never orders, customers or carts. Hidden products don't appear. Cart and checkout remain dynamic store pages.

Templates

With a block theme, WordPress builds every page of the site starting from a template: one for posts, one for pages, one for archives, one for the 404 page and so on. Template parts are the pieces shared by several templates, such as the header and the footer. In Histeon, templates and parts are edited in the builder exactly like a page.

What you need #

  • A block theme. Histeon works on the templates of the active theme; with a classic theme the Templates item doesn't appear.
  • The right permissions. You need access to the builder and permission to change the site's appearance (usually administrators).

The recommended theme is Fondamenta, distributed with Histeon (see below). Another block theme works too: its templates open in the builder and the WordPress blocks they contain stay where they are.

Where to find them #

There are three ways to open a template.

WhereHow
Histeon → Templates in wp-adminThe list of the active theme's templates and parts, divided into groups
The builder's Templates sectionThe same list inside the builder, with group filters and the Search for a template… search
Admin bar on the siteWhile you are looking at a page, Edit template with Histeon: … opens the template that page uses (if it has already been customized)

The groups follow WordPress's logic: General (index, home, front page, 404, search), Pages, Posts, Archives, a group for each custom content type (for example "Events", with the single item and the archive), Categories, tags and taxonomies, custom templates and, for parts, Headers, Footer and the other parts. Each item has a line explaining where it applies.

Theme templates and customized templates #

Each item carries a label:

  • Theme file: the template exists only as a theme file. In wp-admin the button is Edit with Histeon.
  • Custom: the template has already been copied into the database. The button is Open in Histeon.

The first time you open a "theme file" template, Histeon makes a copy of it in the database and opens that copy, as the WordPress Site Editor does when you customize a template. It happens only once; the theme file is not touched.

Creating a template #

In Histeon → Templates open New template. From the builder's Templates section the same panel opens with + New template, in a new tab.

  1. Choose the type: Full template or Template part. For a part, also choose the area: general, header or footer.
  2. Type the technical name. The field suggests the WordPress template hierarchy names the theme doesn't have yet: for example single-event for every single event, archive-event for the list of events, category, search, 404.
  3. Type a title, if you like, and choose where to begin in Start from: empty, or a copy of an existing template.
  4. Press Create and open in Histeon: the new template opens in the builder.

A hierarchy name applies by itself where expected. Any other name creates a custom template: you assign it to pages and posts from their editor, in the template panel.

Editing a template #

A template is built like a page: elements, classes, components, loops and conditions (see the chapter on content and data). Two differences matter.

The data comes from the page being visited. In a template, this is the content the visitor is looking at: {this.title} in the post template becomes the title of each post. In an archive you use the page's query:

{#loop main() as post}
  <article>
    <h2><a href={post.url}>{post.title}</a></h2>
    {#if post.excerpt}<p>{post.excerpt}</p>{/if}
  </article>
{/loop}

The pages of an archive. WordPress splits an archive into pages (how many items per page is set in Settings → Reading). In an archive template props.page tells you where you are and already gives the right addresses: props.page.page is the page shown, props.page.totalPages how many there are, props.page.totalRecords how many items the archive has, props.page.prevUrl and props.page.nextUrl the addresses of the previous and next page (empty when there is none), props.page.pageLinks one entry per page with number, url and current:

<nav aria-label="Pagination">
  {#if props.page.prevUrl}<a href={props.page.prevUrl}>Previous</a>{/if}
  {#loop props.page.pageLinks as l}
    {#if l.current}<span aria-current="page">{l.number}</span>{:else}<a href={l.url}>{l.number}</a>{/if}
  {/loop}
  {#if props.page.nextUrl}<a href={props.page.nextUrl}>Next</a>{/if}
</nav>

The addresses follow the site's permalinks (/books/page/2/, or ?paged=2 with plain permalinks) and work on the archives of types and terms, the posts page and the author and date archives.

The content written in the editor has its own place. In post and page templates, the Post content element (Add menu, or {@content} in the HTML panel) marks where the text that editors write in the block editor goes.

Every template has its Post content, list templates too. When a template lacks it, the builder says so in a strip above the canvas while you edit it: with Histeon Publish a template without this block does not travel, and reading and sending the site stop on it. The Add the post content button adds it (where you selected, or at the end): move it where the text goes and save. On a list template (archives, search, 404) it stays empty and does not show, on the site as in Histeon Publish. The warning blocks nothing: you can save and publish anyway. Template parts do not need it.

In the builder's top bar, next to the name, Template or Template part appears, so you always know what you are editing.

Template parts #

Header and footer are documents of their own: they open from the same list and are edited once for all the pages that use them. In the Fondamenta templates they are pulled in with WordPress's "template part" block, which the builder keeps as it is.

Preview with sample content #

A template has no page of its own: to show it, the canvas and the preview use sample content.

TemplateSample content
Single post, single item of a type (single, single-event…)A published item of that type
Page, front page, template partsA page
Archives, index, home, search, 404A list: the latest posts

Above the canvas, the Preview with menu chooses the sample content among the latest 20 published items you can read. For lists, the note list of the latest posts appears instead. Changing the sample is useful to see how the template behaves with a long title, without an image or with many categories.

The Preview button in the top bar also opens the real page on the chosen sample content.

A template's preview link opens any page of the site with the changes in development, because a template applies to many addresses. Share it only with people who need to see the not-yet-published version.

Saving and publishing #

Templates and parts work like any Histeon document:

  • Save (Ctrl+S) saves them to development: the public site doesn't change.
  • Publish… takes them live with a release, together with the other changes.
  • You restore an earlier version from the document's history.

If someone edits a template outside Histeon, for example in the WordPress Site Editor, the change still goes into the document's history, as with pages. For releases, restores and history, see the chapter on versions and releases.

The Fondamenta theme #

Fondamenta is the base block theme designed for Histeon. With Histeon active it adds nothing of its own: everything you see is drawn by Histeon.

  • No presets: no WordPress palettes, gradients, font sizes or patterns getting in the way of your CSS framework.
  • No remote patterns downloaded from the WordPress library.
  • Four templates already written with Histeon elements: index (the list of posts), singular (posts and single items), page and 404. All of them have the "Post content" block, index and 404 too, where it stays empty: so every template travels with Histeon Publish.
  • Two parts: header, with the site name and the navigation menu, and footer.

It is the recommended starting point: open the Templates section, customize the header and footer, then the template for single items and the one for lists.

Need an extra template, for example the one for a content type of your own? Create it with New template (see above).

The name doesn't contain "Histeon" because the theme stays afterwards: it is the base on which a site will one day be able to leave Histeon for blocks that work on their own. Without Histeon Fondamenta is "stopped": the pages show nothing, and in wp-admin a notice says which plugin is missing, how many contents are left without a page and which content types are not registered. For now the fix is to activate Histeon again.

From XB Blank to Fondamenta #

XB Blank stays as a compatibility theme, with the same templates as Fondamenta: a site that uses it keeps looking as before. On the Themes and Dashboard screens a notice suggests the switch.

  1. Make a backup of the site.
  2. Upload fondamenta-<version>-<commit>.zip from the same Histeon release via Appearance → Themes → Add New Theme → Upload Theme.
  3. Activate Fondamenta (from the notice or from the Themes screen).

When the theme changes, Histeon carries to Fondamenta the templates, parts, global styles and additional CSS you customized with XB Blank: they stay the same documents, with their history. If Fondamenta already has a template with the same name, Fondamenta's stays and the Log says so. Activating XB Blank again brings everything back.

The exit report #

If one day a site has to leave Histeon, its pages will become Fondamenta blocks for the structure and WordPress blocks for the text. To know beforehand how much would come out of it, an administrator can read a report from WP-CLI:

wp xb export-core --user=<administrator>

It changes nothing. For every page, template and component it says what would become an editable block, what would stay as it is today in an HTML block (for example an SVG or a list of categories), what would be lost (for example a Histeon form or a condition on the visitor) and why. At the end, the share of elements that become real blocks. With --file=report.json it saves everything to a file, with --doc=<id> it reads a single document.

Fondamenta's blocks #

Since Fondamenta 0.2 the theme has the blocks pages will be converted into. You also find them in the block editor's inserter:

  • Element (with the variations Section, Container, Image, Link, Button): an HTML element with its tag and classes, holding other blocks, a text or nothing. The markup stays the one you built, so your CSS keeps working. An element can show values of the content: the title, the date, a field, the link of a post;
  • Loop: repeats its blocks for each post of a query, each term, each value of a field or each page of an archive; If the loop is empty says what to show when there are no items;
  • If present: shows its blocks only if a value is there, or if a comparison holds (for example the menu item of the section you are in);
  • Post content.

To see how a converted page would look, without touching it:

wp xb export-core --user=<administrator> --compare

For every page, template and part it draws the page twice, with Histeon and with the converted blocks, and says whether the HTML is the same. Components become WordPress synced patterns when they can: the definition stays one, and each use keeps its own texts and links as its own values. A component with slots, or with props in a class, a condition or a loop, is instead copied into every page that uses it. Loop filters (search, topics, sorting, pages) move to Fondamenta too: their definitions go into the site theme and pages filter as before, even with Histeon switched off. Content fields, repeaters included, move to the field plugin you choose (Secure Custom Fields, ACF, Meta Box, Pods, JetEngine or Open Fields): --theme-zip=theme.zip --campi=scf puts the field groups into the site theme, and pages show them as before. The static site's forms (those posting to /xb-form) become forms of the plugin you choose too (Fluent Forms, Contact Form 7, Forminator, JetFormBuilder, Ninja Forms or SureForms), with the messages already received: --forms --form=fluentforms shows what they would become.

When the site is ready, the exit is made from Histeon → Settings → Exit to Gutenberg: choose the field plugin and the form plugin, look at the preview, download and install the site theme, then «Write the exit» (with a full copy of the site made first). Pages are converted in place, with a backup: «Restore» puts them back as they were. After checking the pages, «Finish the exit» activates the site theme and turns Histeon off, without deleting it. From then on do not publish Histeon releases any more. With --convert --doc=<id> the command shows the converted blocks of one document, without writing into the contents: «Write the exit» is what writes them.

The site theme #

On the day of the exit, a theme made for your site goes on top of Fondamenta: its stylesheet, its templates and parts already converted, the scripts of its pages. It is generated with:

wp xb export-core --user=<administrator> --theme-zip=theme.zip

It changes nothing on the site: it only writes the ZIP file, to upload by hand in Appearance → Themes → Add New Theme → Upload Theme when the time comes. The theme is named after your site and, once installed, it is yours: you edit it in the Site Editor or in its files. If Histeon is ever switched off, Fondamenta still registers your content types with the same addresses and keeps the interactions (tabs, menus, reveal) working.

Interactions and code

Many pages need something that moves: tabs, FAQs that open, a menu for phones. Histeon offers these interactions ready-made, without writing code. When you need more, you can add JavaScript to the page or use your theme's PHP. This chapter also covers static site forms and page review by clients.

Interactions without code #

In the Add menu (Ctrl+I), Interactions group, you find:

InteractionWhat it doesWithout JavaScript
TabsButtons that show one panel at a time. The arrows, Home and End move from one tab to another.All panels visible, one below the other
AccordionQuestions and answers: one section open at a time. It uses the browser's native element, so keyboard and screen readers work on their own.Works the same: it uses no script
Mobile menuA Menu button opens and closes the navigation on narrow screens; Esc closes it and returns focus to the button.Menu always open
Reveal on scrollThe block appears when it comes into view.Everything visible; with reduced motion turned on, visible right away
Language switcherLinks to the page's other languages, if the site is multilingual.Works the same

Each insertion is normal Histeon HTML, with BEM-style classes (tabs, tabs__tab, accordion__item…) and its base styles. You can change text, structure and appearance as for any other element. Base styles are added only to classes that don't have a style on the site yet, so a second insertion doesn't overwrite what you have already customized.

Scripts don't run on the builder canvas: tabs and menus are shown open, so every part stays editable. You try out the behavior with Preview.

The interactions script weighs a few KB and loads only on pages that use at least one of them. It reads only the interactions' attributes: it never runs anything written in the page.

Page JavaScript #

Every page, template or component can have its own script, in the builder's JavaScript panel. The script goes into the page as <script type="module"> after the content.

  • It doesn't run on the canvas or in the builder's preview. You see it in action on the published page, or by opening the preview in a New tab.
  • It is saved and published with the document, and it is versioned like everything else.
  • A component's script reaches every page that uses that component.

Who can write code #

JavaScript is the most sensitive content on a site: whoever can write it can do anything in the visitor's browser, administrators included. That is why Histeon follows WordPress's rule:

  • Only those with the unfiltered_html permission can write or edit JavaScript. In a standard installation, administrators have it (and, on multisite, only super admins).
  • Others see the panel read-only, with the note Read-only: only users with the unfiltered_html permission can edit the JavaScript.
  • In the same way, the markup of those without that permission is sanitized on save, as WordPress does with authors' content.
  • Trust isn't "recycled". If an editor without unfiltered_html edits a page and then an administrator saves or publishes it, that content is sanitized again: an administrator doesn't make it trusted just by pressing a button.
  • Protected preview. When the development version contains untrusted content, the preview runs with scripts blocked.

The same rule applies when you import a release from another site: those without unfiltered_html get sanitized markup and no JavaScript.

Site scripts #

A library or file the whole site needs (a map, a booking form, an analytics tool) is added once: in the JavaScript panel, open Site scripts and press Add a script. For each script choose:

  • File address: a file of this site (for example /wp-content/uploads/js/map.js) or an https address.
  • Integrity hash: required for a file of another site (sha384-…, usually shown next to the library's link). If that file changes, the browser does not run it.
  • Where: the whole site, or only the pages of one content type (and its archive).
  • Position (head or end of the page) and Loading: defer (after the page, the usual choice), async, module or right away (blocks the page until it arrives: only when really needed).

Site scripts are saved in development and reach the site with the next release, like the settings. The rules of page JavaScript apply: only unfiltered_html changes and publishes them, an import or an agent never touches them, and they do not run in the builder.

PHP: in the theme files, not in the editor #

Histeon never runs PHP written in the builder, in any form. The reason is simple: a stolen builder account, a release imported from another site or a preview link must not be able to run code on the server. The right permission for writing PHP is the one for editing theme files, which many sites keep turned off, and Histeon doesn't reopen it through another door.

PHP is therefore written in a file of the theme (for example functions.php) or of a plugin, where it has its own defenses: it is versioned, reviewed, saved with backups. Pages use it in three safe ways.

WhatIn PHPOn the page
DataThe xb_data_providers filter registers a function with a name{data.weather.today} or {#loop data.weather.days as d}
ModifiersThe xb_modifiers filter registers a transformation{item.product.price.euro()}
Dynamic blocksregister_block_type with a render functionAdd menu, Site PHP blocks group

Example of a modifier, to put in the theme's functions.php:

add_filter( 'xb_modifiers', static fn ( array $m ): array => $m + array(
	'euro' => static fn ( mixed $v ): string => number_format( (float) $v, 2, ',', '.' ) . ' €',
) );

The rules that protect the site:

  • Data values come out sanitized like any expression: a function can't print HTML through {data.…}.
  • Content, terms and users returned by a function follow the usual visibility rules: a draft stays hidden from visitors.
  • An error in the function leaves the value empty; the page stays up.
  • A dynamic block produces the HTML its code decides, with the same trust as a theme template.

In the HTML panel, inside {…}, after data. the completion suggests the data registered by the site; after another dot, Ctrl+Space suggests the modifiers.

Builder JavaScript API #

For people who build tools or automations, the builder page exposes window.xb: the same operations as the builder's controls, on the same state. It doesn't grant new permissions: every save goes through the same filters and the same versions.

MethodWhat it does
getHtml(), setHtml(html)Reads and replaces the text of the HTML panel
getSelection(), select(uid)Selection
insert(html), remove(uid)Adds inside or after the selection, removes an element
getCss(selector), setCss(selector, css)CSS of a style entry
render()The page's HTML and CSS as the server renders them
undo(), redo(), save(), isDirty()Like the builder's commands
on(event, fn)Listens for an event: change, select or save
xb.on('select', ({ uid }) => console.log(xb.getElementHtml(uid)));
xb.insert('<p class="note">Added by a tool</p>');
await xb.save();

Forms with Fluent Forms #

Histeon doesn't build forms: for contacts, sign-ups and quotes it uses Fluent Forms, when it is active on the site, right from the builder.

  1. In the Add menu (Ctrl+I) you find the Forms (Fluent Forms) group with the site's forms: pick one and it appears in the page, drawn by Fluent Forms as on the site.
  2. Select the form and, in the Properties panel, press Edit the form: the Fluent Forms editor opens inside the builder. There you change fields, messages and notifications.
  3. Close and refresh the preview takes you back to the page, with the updated form.
  4. New Fluent Forms form…, in the same menu group, creates a form without leaving the builder.

Submissions, notifications and permissions stay Fluent Forms': the group appears only to those who can manage forms in Fluent Forms. On a static site, Fluent Forms forms work with Static Bridge.

Static site forms #

A static site has no server to receive forms. Histeon solves this for sites published on Cloudflare: the form sends the data to the site's Worker, which checks it and forwards it to WordPress.

How to build a form:

  1. The form submits with method="post" to the /xb-form address.
  2. It contains the Cloudflare Turnstile widget (the anti-bot check), with your site key.
  3. Two optional hidden fields: xb_form, the name of the form (for example contact), and xb_redirect, the page of the site to take the sender to (for example /thank-you/).
<form method="post" action="/xb-form">
  <input type="hidden" name="xb_form" value="contact">
  <input type="hidden" name="xb_redirect" value="/thank-you/">
  <label>Name <input name="name" required></label>
  <label>Email <input name="email" type="email" required></label>
  <label>Message <textarea name="message"></textarea></label>
  <div class="cf-turnstile" data-sitekey="YOUR-SITE-KEY"></div>
  <button>Send</button>
</form>

The Turnstile widget needs Cloudflare's script. If your site doesn't load it already, you can add it from the page's JavaScript panel:

const s = document.createElement('script');
s.src = 'https://challenges.cloudflare.com/turnstile/v0/api.js';
s.async = true;
document.head.append(s);

What happens on each submission:

  • the Worker verifies Turnstile, keeps at most 50 fields and redirects only to pages of the same site;
  • WordPress accepts only recent submissions signed by the Worker, and rejects duplicates;
  • the message appears in wp-admin in Form messages, visible only to administrators, and an email is sent to the site administrator's address;
  • messages are personal data: they are deleted after 180 days and are covered by WordPress's privacy tools (export and erasure).

The Turnstile secret key is entered in Histeon → Settings → Static site, Static site forms (Turnstile) section, with Send to the Worker: it is sent to Cloudflare and doesn't stay on the site.

The /xb-form path exists only on the static site published on Cloudflare. On the dynamic WordPress site, use a forms plugin. See also the chapter on the static site.

Review and reviewer comments #

A client or a colleague can comment on a page before it goes live, without a WordPress account.

  1. In the builder press Preview, then New tab: the address that opens is a signed, expiring preview link.
  2. Send that link to whoever needs to review.
  3. Whoever opens the link sees the Review bar at the bottom left:
    • Comment: hovering over the page highlights the elements (Pick an element… (Esc)); a click opens the box, where you write the comment and your name, then Send;
    • Approve: asks for a name and records the approval of the version being viewed.

In the builder, the Comments section gathers the document's comments, split into To do and Resolved, with author and date. Show the element selects the commented element; then Resolved, Reopen or Delete. The section's icon shows how many comments are to do.

The approval appears at the top of the Comments and in the Publish window. It remembers the version that was seen: if you changed the page after the approval, the builder says so and it's worth asking for a new one.

The preview link is the reviewer's credential: whoever has it can comment on that document as long as the link is valid. If the content in development was written by someone without unfiltered_html, the preview runs with scripts blocked and the review bar doesn't appear.

Comments accept only plain text (at most 2000 characters) and have limits against repeated submissions. Reviewers' names are personal data and are deleted together with the comment.

Media and fonts

Images and fonts weigh more than everything else on a page.
